# idealingua-v1 modernization — Task Ledger

Authoritative ledger of planned and completed work. Master plan: `./docs/drafts/20260503-1200-modernization-plan.md`.

The deliverable of this session is **plan documents only**, not implementation. Each PR below produces one plan doc that future implementation work will execute against.

Status: `[ ]` planned · `[~]` in progress · `[x]` done · `[!]` blocked

---

- **X1: v1.4.19-compatible corpus subset + clean regression baseline (Scala/TS/C#)** (2026-05-14) — R4 documented that v1.4.19's idlc backends (`ScalaTypeConverter.scala:80`, `TypeScriptTypeConverter.scala:325`, `CSharpImports.scala:80`) cannot translate the post-v1.4.19 corpus features added by R2 (`coverage/*.domain`) plus the F5-fix `idltest/blobtest.domain` (TBLOB → Array[Byte]). The full-corpus R4 baselines therefore record `exit_kind=build-failure-old-side` and are unable to surface wire-format regressions. X1 builds a v1.4.19-compatible corpus subset and re-runs the regression harness for all 3 languages. **Corpus:** `regression-harness/selftest-corpus/main-tests-v1419-compat/source/` (28 .domain files + 5 .model files), built via per-file symlinks back into `main-tests/source/`, EXCLUDING the 10 R2-added `coverage/*.domain` fixtures AND `idltest/blobtest.domain`. The symlink layout matches `main-tests/`'s relative paths so `Harness.scala:hashIdlTree` produces a deterministic `idl_sha256=d80b8ae8f6c33e4d9b2fb47ff52882d334f2de8984f381ee79537285c5e57021`. **Sample apps:** filtered copies of R3a/R3b/R3c full-corpus sample apps with all `coverage.*` and `idltest.blobtest.*` wireId entries dropped — 339 (scala) + 182 (ts) + 295 (cs) entries retained from the originals' 393 + 201 + 327. Generator script: `debug/20260514-x1-build-v1419-compat-corpus.py` (3-step: build symlink tree, hash, filter sample apps). **v1.4.19-compat audit of R2 fixtures:** all 10 `coverage/*.domain` fixtures fail under v1.4.19 (per R4 baseline diagnosis — `ScalaTypeConverter.scala:80 IDLException: No type->generic mappings implemented yet`); `idltest/blobtest.domain` ALSO fails for the same generic-mapping reason (TBLOB → Array[Byte] is a generic-mapping path introduced in commit 14c62ca PR-02 F5-fix, post-v1.4.19); all other 28 `.domain` files compile cleanly under v1.4.19. **Per-language results:**
  - **Scala** (`v1419-vs-head-compat-scala`): v1.4.19's idlc compiles the corpus (28 domains → 214 .scala files); HEAD's idlc matches; sample-app build against v1.4.19-generated code **FAILS with 16 scala-cli `type mismatch` errors** at sample_app.scala lines 897/1353/1361/1369, all involving `izumi.test.domain02.DTO1`. Root-cause: **case-class field-order regression**. v1.4.19 emits `final case class DTO1(if1Field_overriden, if1Field_inherited, fromOtherDomain, fromOtherDomainDirect, if3Field, if2Field, sameField, sameEverywhereField)` while HEAD emits `(if1Field_overriden, if1Field_inherited, sameField, sameEverywhereField, fromOtherDomain, fromOtherDomainDirect, if3Field, if2Field)`. Same field SET, different ORDER. Affects 4 wireIds (`DTO1`, `SomeResp.DTO1`, `TestAliasServ.TestADTIdImportedReturnOutput.DTO1`, `TestAliasServ.TestADTIdReturnOutput.DTO1`). PR-02 IMPL-7a.2-Fe series (ef4a75a..fa11569) is the in-flight refinement of inherited-field ordering; the surfaced regression is consistent with that work direction. Baseline recorded `exit_kind=build-failure-old-side` with the divergence root cause documented.
  - **TypeScript** (`v1419-vs-head-compat-typescript`): **0 divergences across 182 NDJSON lines**, exit 0. The TS sample-app uses object-literal `new DTO1({...})` construction (positional-arg-insensitive), so the DTO1 field-order regression is invisible at the TS wire level (the `serialize()` body emits field-decl order on both v1.4.19 and HEAD identically). This is real wire-format-invariant evidence: TS-emitted wire bytes for the v1.4.19-compatible corpus are byte-stable across v1.4.19 → HEAD.
  - **C#** (`v1419-vs-head-compat-csharp`): v1.4.19's idlc compiles the corpus (28 domains → 269 .cs files); HEAD's idlc matches; **dotnet build FAILS with 16 CS1503 `cannot convert from 'long' to TestValIdentifier`/vice-versa errors** at sample_app.cs lines 302/315/319/321 — same 4 DTO1-positional-ctor invocations as Scala (same regression, different rendering target). The TS leg passes because object-literal; Scala+C# both fail because positional. The pre-existing C# adapter stdout/stderr split defect (R4) does NOT surface here because the build fails BEFORE the sample app runs.
  **Real findings (1):** DTO1 case-class field-order regression between v1.4.19 and HEAD — TestInterface2+TestInterface3 inheritance flattening produces a different field sequence. The wire-format gate fires only on positional-ctor languages (Scala, C#); object-literal-language (TS) emits identical bytes. Worth tracking against PR-02 IMPL-7a.2-Fe completion. **Baselines committed:** `regression-harness/selftest-expectations/v1419-vs-head.main-tests-v1419-compat.{scala,typescript,csharp}.json`. **selftest.sh shortcuts added:** `v1419-vs-head-compat-{scala,typescript,csharp}`. **Surprises:** (a) blobtest.domain was added POST-v1.4.19 in commit 14c62ca (PR-02 F5-fix) and uses TBLOB which trips the same `ScalaTypeConverter.scala:80 IDLException` as the R2 generics fixtures — initially missed because R4's stop-condition comment attributed the failure to R2 alone; the v1419-compat corpus had to exclude blobtest in addition to coverage/* on the first iteration. (b) The R4 baselines on the FULL corpus stop at the idlc-build-old-side stage (NotImplementedError), so they never reached the sample-app build and never saw the DTO1 regression; X1's narrower corpus is what surfaces it. (c) TS leg PASSING is significant: it demonstrates that under TS's object-literal serialization, the field-order regression is wire-invisible, which has implications for how the cross-language wire-format gate should weight per-language signals.

- **R4: regression-harness v1.4.19 vs HEAD on full main-tests corpus (baselines committed)** (2026-05-14) — R3-fixups landed first (see entry below); R4 then ran `./regression-harness/idl-regress --project <main-tests> --old git:v1.4.19 --new self --lang {scala,typescript,csharp}` for all three languages. **All three exit=2 (build-failure-old-side) with `NotImplementedError` from v1.4.19's idlc backends** when fed the post-v1.4.19 corpus types (the R2 expansion: `coverage/generics-blob.domain` + `coverage/generics-map-keys.domain` + `coverage/generics-nested.domain` + recursive-ADT shapes in `idltest/algebraics`+`idltest/ast`+`idltest/upcasts`). Per-language failure sites: Scala → `ScalaTypeConverter.scala:80` (`IDLException: No type->generic mappings implemented yet`); TypeScript → `TypeScriptTypeConverter.scala:325 toFieldMember`; C# → `CSharpImports.scala:80 withImport`. **Baselines committed** under `regression-harness/selftest-expectations/v1419-vs-head.main-tests.{scala,typescript,csharp}.json` recording exit_kind/exit_code/failure_site/failure_message/version_gap_class — future R4 reruns must observe the same failure mode; a change in mode is an explicit signal that the operator must re-baseline. This is the documented version-gap class (R2 expanded the corpus beyond what v1.4.19's idlc backends support); the wire-format invariant against v1.4.19 stays measurable via the `broad` selftest corpus (see `v1419-vs-head.broad.scala.json`, divergences=[]). **Substantive findings (3)**: (i) v1.4.19's Scala backend lacks generic-type→Scala mapping (`???` at converter:80); (ii) v1.4.19's TS backend `toFieldMember` is missing post-R2 field-shape branches; (iii) v1.4.19's C# backend `CSharpImports.withImport` is similarly incomplete. None are regressions in HEAD — HEAD compiles the corpus cleanly via the new typer + ported renderers. The **C# adapter has a separate pre-existing canonicalize defect** (`dotnet run` emits SYSLIB0014 deprecation warnings to stdout, which the NDJSON parser misreads) surfaced by the smoke test on `dtofields-only` against bare `b6807c8` — documented in the C# baseline file as F-followup-csharp-dotnet-stdout-stderr-split, NOT introduced by R3-fixups. Verification matrix: 3 FROZEN harness contracts × 2 Scala versions = 6 lines green (2.13.18 + 3.8.3 each: `runWireFixtures` 26 Scala + 20 TypeScript + 21 CSharp fixtures match, `runCrossLangInterop` 118 verified / 10 skipped / 6 excluded, `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test` 48/48). Smoke tests of the regression-harness selftest matrix against `dtofields-only`: Scala sanity = 0 divergences over 393 lines (confirms A1 fix — `izumi/test/...` now compiled by scala-cli); TS sanity = 0 divergences over 3 lines (confirms A2 fix — moment import works under tsx without esModuleInterop cascade); C# sanity blocked by the pre-existing canonicalize defect above (also fails on bare `b6807c8`, so not introduced by R3-fixups). Status: **clean v1.4.19→HEAD known-divergence inventory documented**; substantive wire-format regression vs v1.4.19 not measurable on the FULL corpus due to the version-gap (would require porting the post-v1.4.19 corpus features back into v1.4.19's idlc backends, which is the wrong direction). The narrower `broad`-corpus baseline at zero divergences is the available wire-format-invariant check vs v1.4.19. R4 deliverable per brief: "we have a baseline" — landed.

- **R3-fixups: ScalaAdapter test-dir handling + IRT moment-import fix** (2026-05-14) — Two surgical fixes prerequisite to R4. **A1 (ScalaAdapter test-dir auto-exclude)**: `scala-cli run <workDir>` silently skips any source under a directory literally named `test/` (part of `--default-forbidden-directories`, hidden flag — see `scala-cli run --help-full`). The corpus contains `izumi/test/*.domain` which generates output under `generated/izumi/test/...` and was being silently dropped, defeating the Scala leg for ~5 domains. Picked option (a) explicit file enumeration over option (b) `--exclude` (which is the opposite direction — `--exclude` removes inputs, doesn't add forbidden-dir-excluded ones back) and option (c) directory rename (invasive — touches generated import paths). `ScalaAdapter.buildAndRun` now walks `workDir` for every `.scala` file (lexicographically sorted for reproducibility) and passes them individually to scala-cli; scala-cli treats explicit-file inputs as accepted regardless of containing-directory name. Reproduced the original defect on `/tmp/sccli-test/foo/test/Main.scala` (`scala-cli run .` → "No main class found"; same call with explicit file → "HELLO FROM TEST DIR"). +15 LOC production (`listScalaInputs` helper + invocation change), 0 LOC tests (verified end-to-end via sanity-scala selftest). **A2 (IRT formatter.ts moment-import F13)**: `idealingua-v1-runtime-rpc-typescript/src/main/resources/runtime/typescript/irt/formatter.ts:1-2` used `import * as moment from 'moment'` then called `moment(value, …)` — under tsx/esbuild's CJS transform `__toESM(require("moment"))` wraps the callable in a non-callable namespace object (`Object.create(Function.prototype)`), throwing `TypeError: moment is not a function` on every Date round-trip. The brief suggested switching to `import moment from 'moment'` + flipping `esModuleInterop: true` in the harness tsconfig, but **changed to `import moment = require('moment')` instead** — the TS-idiomatic form for `export = moment` (which is what moment v2's `.d.ts` declares; verified via `node_modules/moment/moment.d.ts:end`). The `import = require` form works WITHOUT `esModuleInterop` and WITHOUT `allowSyntheticDefaultImports`, so it does NOT cascade into the downstream `idealingua-v1-runtime-rpc-typescript/src/npmjs/tsconfig.json` (the publishable tsconfig consumers depend on — `esModuleInterop` is false there) and does NOT require flipping the regression-harness `tsconfig.json` either. Verified via local smoke test (`/tmp/moment-smoke/probe.ts` with `import moment = require('moment')` + tsconfig esModuleInterop=false → tsx exit 0, `OK 2025-01-01`). +1 LOC change + 5 LOC explanatory comment to `formatter.ts`. **Did NOT update `FixtureSeeder.ts`** (the dev tool documents F13 as a known limitation that no longer applies — but adding the AllTypes.Struct seeding code would require authoring a new fixture + canonical bytes, which is feature-work not fix-work; surgical-changes principle keeps it untouched). **Did NOT expand R3b's TS sample app to include the 12 timestamp-bearing wireIds** — the existing 162-line skip list in `sample_app.ts` is dominated by service/buzzer ephemerals not exported in TS (issues α/β/δ/ε from R3b), not by F13; only `AllTypes.Struct`/`AllTypesObject` etc. would gain emission lines, and those depend on the polymorphic-mixin skip class (ε) which is independent of moment. Verification: `sbt '++ 2.13.18; runWireFixtures; runCrossLangInterop; idealingua-v1-test-harness/test'` + `sbt '++ 3.8.3; …'` → 6/6 FROZEN harness lines green (26+20+21 wire-fixture totals, 118/10/6 cross-lang totals, 48/48 test totals on each Scala version); `regression-harness/selftest.sh sanity-scala` 0 divergences over 393 lines (confirms A1); `sanity-typescript` 0 divergences over 3 lines (confirms A2). Regression-harness scala-cli compile clean.

- **R3c: full-corpus C# sample app for regression-harness** (2026-05-14) — Authored `idealingua-v1/idealingua-v1-test-defs/src/main/resources/defs/main-tests/.idl-regression/sample_app.cs` (368 LOC) + meta sidecar `sample_app.cs.meta` carrying `idl_sha256=c4c80705ac780e3b782e41350624e9a19ed3936c8613fdebf9a37c7f37593511` (same as R3a/R3b). Covers **327 user-declared C# types as positional `new <FQ>(...)` invocations**, each serialized via `JsonConvert.SerializeObject(...)` with a `StringEnumConverter` added to a `JsonSerializerSettings` (the idealingua C# enum types do not carry a `[JsonConverter]` attribute on the enum decl, so Newtonsoft's default is integer-emission; the explicit StringEnumConverter restores the name-as-string convention that the generated converters' `WriteJson` uses). Output is a `SortedDictionary<string,string>(StringComparer.Ordinal)` keyed by `"<wireId>\t<scenario>"` → flushed line by line on stdout. **C#-specific surprises** (the brief expected R3a-Scala-style positional ctors and got them — but downstream:):
  - **(α) Per-method service+buzzer ephemerals use a different naming convention than Scala.** The Scala `:scala` translator emits `<Method>Input` / `<Method>Output` as top-level companion case classes; the C# `:csharp` translator emits inputs prefixed `In<Method>` (e.g. `InHello`, `InUserRegistered`, `InEnumInput`) and outputs prefixed `Out<Method>` (e.g. `OutImplicitStruct`, `OutAlgebraicEvent`) as nested classes inside the static service/buzzer wrapper (`Idltest.Events.TestBuzzer.InHello` rather than `idltest.events.TestBuzzer.HelloInput`). Furthermore the C# tree DOES export these (unlike TS where they were private to the file). R3c therefore emits wireIds **as the C# translator names them** — the wireId convention is "lowercase package-prefix + PascalCase outer + class name" derived from the file's `RTTI_FULLCLASSNAME` constant where present, falling back to a name-mangling of `Namespace.Outer.Class`. R3c's wireIds for service/buzzer ephemerals therefore differ from R3a's — `coverage.buzzers.alloutputs.AllOutputsBuzzer.OutAlgebraicEvent` (C#) vs `coverage.buzzers.alloutputs.AllOutputsBuzzer.AlgebraicEventOutput` (Scala). The regression-harness compares the SAME adapter's output between v1.4.19 and HEAD per-language, so the cross-language wireId divergence is not a correctness issue — each language has its own canonical wireId set.
  - **(β) Driver.csproj template required broadening to include IRT/Transport interfaces.** The pre-R3c template (committed at `regression-harness M5`) excluded ALL of `csharp/IRT/**/*.cs` from compilation and only re-included `Marshaller/`, `Logger/`, and a handful of root files (`Dispatcher.cs`, `Either.cs`, etc.). The selftest `dtofields-only` corpus had no service/buzzer types, so the exclusion was invisible. The full corpus has service-Client classes (`AllOutputsServiceClientGeneric<C>`, `TestBuzzerClientGeneric<C,D>`, ...) that reference `IClientTransport<C>`, `IClientTransportContext`, `IClientSocketTransport<C,D>`, `IClientTransportCallback`, and `ClientTransportCallback<T>` — all defined under `csharp/IRT/Transport/Client/`. R3c extends the csproj template to add `<Compile Include="csharp/IRT/Transport/Authorization/**/*.cs" />` + the four Client interface files + `ClientTransportCallback.cs` + `SyncHttpTransport.cs`/`SyncHttpTransportGeneric.cs`/`AsyncHttpTransport.cs`/`AsyncHttpTransportGeneric.cs` (the service ClientGeneric factory `SetHTTPTransport` instantiates these directly, so they must be in the compilation unit) + `TransportException.cs`. Continued exclusions: `WebSocketTransport*.cs` and the `Server/WebSocket*` files (they import `WebSocketSharp` which is not a net9.0-supplied package), `UrlEscaper.cs` (depends on `System.Web`). The selftest dtofields-only build still passes with this widened csproj (verified `dotnet run --project /tmp/selftest-build` → exit 0, 3 expected NDJSON lines).
  - **(γ) IRT.Transport.UrlEscaper shim in sample_app.cs.** The C#-generated Identifier types' `_JsonNetConverter.WriteJson` calls `value.ToString()` which calls `IRT.Transport.UrlEscaper.Escape(...)`. The generated `csharp/IRT/UrlEscaper.cs` uses `System.Web.HttpUtility.UrlEncode` — `System.Web` is not auto-referenced in net9.0 (it's a legacy framework dependency). Rather than add a `<PackageReference Include="System.Web">` to the csproj (which would not resolve cleanly on .NET 9 anyway), R3c declares a minimal shim `namespace IRT.Transport { public static class UrlEscaper { Escape, UnEscape } }` at the top of `sample_app.cs` using `System.Net.WebUtility.UrlEncode/UrlDecode` (a net9.0-built-in). The csproj continues to exclude `IRT/UrlEscaper.cs` so there is no symbol clash. Identifier round-trip via `.ToString()` produces standard URL-encoded names (e.g. `"AdtTestID#s1"`, `"BucketID#3a7f0c12-1234-5678-9abc-fedcba987654:s1:3a7f0c12-1234-5678-9abc-fedcba987654"`).
  - **(δ) Identifier ctor arguments using `@`-prefixed reserved C# keywords.** `Coverage.Usertypes.Idsingle.UsesId` has a generated ctor `public UsesId(Coverage.Usertypes.Idsingle.Singleton @ref)` — the `@` prefix escapes `ref` (a C# reserved keyword) in the parameter name. Initial generator regex `(.+?)\s+(\w+)$` doesn't match `@ref` (because `\w` excludes `@`), so the longest-ctor selection picked the empty-arg ctor `UsesId()` and serialization NPE'd on `v.Ref.ToString()`. Fix: `(.+?)\s+@?(\w+)$` — accept an optional `@` prefix before the parameter name. No other reserved-keyword identifier ctors in the corpus.
  - **(ε) Recursive interface-typed fields (e.g. `Izumi.Test.Domain01.AllTypes` has an `option: AllTypes` field — not wrapped in container).** The recursion terminator is `depth > 4 → fall back to empty-ctor on the resolved struct` (`new AllTypesStruct()`), with the standard 0-arg ctor matching every generated final class. This is functionally equivalent to R3a's depth-4 `List.empty[...] / Map.empty / None / Set.empty` termination — the encoded JSON terminates at 4 levels of nesting with empty / null leaves.
  Recursion-guard policy: collection types (`List<T>`, `HashSet<T>`, `Dictionary<K,V>`) emit empty containers at depth ≥4; user types emit empty-ctor instances at depth >4. ADT branches recurse the wrapped value type one level deeper.
  Verification: `idlc :csharp --root <main-tests> --source <main-tests>/source -t /tmp/cs-out -nz -d layout=PLAIN` → 295 .cs files (255 domain + 40 IRT) over 39 domains; staged `/tmp/csbuild/` with `regression-harness/templates/csharp/Driver.csproj` (rendered with `{{RUNTIME_VERSION}}=1.4.20-SNAPSHOT`) + sample_app.cs + the full generated tree; `dotnet build` succeeded with 2 SYSLIB0014 obsolescence warnings (`WebRequest.Create` in the generated HTTP transports) and 0 errors; `dotnet run --project /tmp/csbuild --verbosity quiet --nologo` → **exit 0, 327 sorted NDJSON lines**. Selftest cross-check: `dtofields-only` corpus regenerated, built against the same widened csproj, ran cleanly → exit 0, 3 lines (`idltest.dtofields.IntPair.Struct zero/mixed`, `idltest.dtofields.WHPair.Struct default`). Sample line shape: `coverage.primitives.AllPrimitivesFlat\tdefault\t{"b_bool":true,"s_str":"s1","i8":1,"i16":1,"i32":1,"i64":2,"u8":1,"u16":1,"u32":1,"u64":2,"f_flt":1.5,"d_dbl":2.5,"uuid":"3a7f0c12-1234-5678-9abc-fedcba987654","blob":"aGkh","ts_zoned":"2025-01-15T10:30:45.000Z","ts_local":"2025-01-15T10:30:45.000","ts_unix":"2025-01-15T10:30:45.000Z","time_only":"10:30:45.000","date_only":"2025-01-15"}`. New defects vs known: **(β)** csproj-template Transport-exclusion was too aggressive for the full corpus — **NOT a code defect, but a regression-harness configuration gap that the broad sample app surfaces for the first time** (selftest was too narrow to catch it). The R2-documented `set[opt[<primitive>]]` C# defect (CS0128/CS0165 duplicate `_<field>_d` local variable) is **NOT triggered** by the current main-tests corpus — `coverage.generics.nested.NestedGenerics` is the only set/opt-bearing type and its set inner type is not `opt[<primitive>]`. Path to R4 (regression-harness run v1.4.19 vs HEAD on full corpus, all 3 languages): with R3a/R3b/R3c sample apps committed, the full-corpus `idl-regress` invocation (`./regression-harness/regress.sh <main-tests-path> --baseline v1.4.19 --head HEAD`) should now produce canonicalized NDJSON for all 3 languages and per-language diffs. Known cross-cutting follow-ups: (i) R3b's `irt/formatter.ts` `moment` import defect (12 timestamp wireIds skipped in TS); (ii) R3a's `scala-cli test/`-dir auto-exclude in `ScalaAdapter.scala`; (iii) the csproj-template broadening (now in `regression-harness/templates/csharp/Driver.csproj`) is committed as part of R3c and is a permanent fix not contingent on R4.

- **R3b: full-corpus TypeScript sample app for regression-harness** (2026-05-14) — Authored `idealingua-v1/idealingua-v1-test-defs/src/main/resources/defs/main-tests/.idl-regression/sample_app.ts` (547 LOC) + meta sidecar carrying the same `idl_sha256=c4c80705…` as R3a. Covers 201 of R3a's 393 wireIds — the wireIds reachable as exported TS classes from the `idlc :typescript` output. **R3b TypeScript-specific surprises** (the brief expected positional ctors; reality differs):
  - **(α) TypeScript-generated DTOs have NO positional constructors.** The IDL→TS translator emits `constructor(data: <Name>Serialized = undefined)` taking a single Serialized-interface argument, not positional args. The regression-gate for TS therefore CAN'T be "positional arg count mismatch ⇒ compile error" the way Scala/C# work — instead it is "Serialized-interface field set mismatch ⇒ TSC compile error". Every emission is `JSON.stringify(new <Cls>(<NameSerialized object literal>).serialize())`. The R3 brief explicitly green-lit this with a `# x-skip-positional` documentation marker; the sample-app header carries the universal `x-skip-positional` note.
  - **(β) Service / buzzer method input+output ephemerals are NOT exported in TS.** The Scala `:scala` translator emits each method's ephemeral input/output as a top-level `final case class <Method>Input/<Method>Output` companion-object case class — 119 such wireIds in R3a (e.g. `coverage.buzzers.alloutputs.AllOutputsBuzzer.AlgebraicEventInput`). The TS `:typescript` translator emits them as **non-exported** classes named with `In*Event` / `Out*Event` prefixes (e.g. `class InAlgebraicEvent` — not `AlgebraicEventInput`), all private to the service-module file. They are unreachable via `import` from `sample_app.ts`. The TS marshaller dispatches input/output payloads via the service+method name pair and never references these wireIds at runtime. R3b skips all 119 — they're documented as `# x-skip-positional` lines at the bottom of `sample_app.ts`.
  - **(γ) TS Identifier toString format-order differs from introspector-field-declaration order.** Multi-field Identifiers like `idltest.identifiers.BucketID(app, user, bucket)` (introspector order from Scala-case-class decl) encode toString as `BucketID#<app>:<bucket>:<user>` (constructor body's `parts[0]=app, parts[1]=bucket, parts[2]=user` decoder chain). The R3b generator extracts BOTH orders: field-decl order from the Introspector block, toString-slot order from `this.<X> = ... parts[N] ...` regex over the file body. Scala positional args (in field-decl order) are then permuted into TS toString slot order before URL-encoding and joining with `:`. Without this remap, the third Scala arg ("s1", a `string`-typed `bucket`) lands in the third TS-toString slot (`user`, UUID-typed) and the `value.match(<uuid-regex>)` validator throws.
  - **(δ) TS Serialized-interface name collisions across modules.** Two unrelated `M2StructSerialized` interfaces exist (in `idltest/aliases2/M2.ts` with `{f2:string}` and in `idltest/clones/M2.ts` with `{value:string, str:string, i32:number}`); a global `Name -> Interface` lookup picks the wrong one and the resolver flattens spurious fields. R3b builds a **qualified `(name, modulePath)` interface index** plus per-file import maps so each interface's `extends` resolves against the importing file's imports, not a global. Likewise, JS class symbols collide (`M2Struct` from both files) — `sample_app.ts` aliases the imports (`M2Struct as M2Struct__idltest_clones_M2`) whenever a single class name maps to >1 module path among the imports.
  - **(ε) Polymorphic mixin fields force a skip.** Any DTO whose Serialized field type is `{[key: string]: <X>StructSerialized}` or `{[key: string]: any} | undefined` (mixin-typed slot — TS encodes the implementing class's FQ as the dict's single key, value is the inner Serialized) cannot be round-tripped from a flat Scala-positional-arg literal because the TS class's constructor calls `<X>Struct.create(data.field)` which looks up the polymorphic registry by the dict key. The generator skips both **direct** polymorphic-field-bearing classes (e.g. `idltest.anyvals.UserData.Struct`) and **transitively** dependent ones (any DTO whose Serialized field type references a polymorphic class's Serialized). 32 directly-affected wireIds + transitively-reachable consumers (AST nodes, `TestOneliners`, `Notification*`, `*WithCovariance`, `AllTypes.Struct`, `AnyValTest2.Struct`, …) all become `# x-skip-positional` lines. Cross-language Layer-C exclusion in `regression-harness/selftest-expectations/` is the right ownership for these.
  - **(F13/ζ) IRT formatter `import * as moment from 'moment'` defect.** `idealingua-v1-runtime-rpc-typescript/src/main/resources/runtime/typescript/irt/formatter.ts:1` uses `import * as moment from 'moment'` then calls `moment(value, Formatter.DATETIME_FORMATS)` — under modern CJS + tsx the namespace import is a non-callable object (the callable is at `.default`); the IRT runtime throws `TypeError: moment is not a function` on every Date round-trip. The brief explicitly named this F13 as a known TS-specific defect. R3b avoids triggering it by skipping any wireId whose Scala R3a expression contains `java.time.(ZonedDateTime|LocalDateTime|LocalTime|LocalDate)` — 12 affected wireIds (`coverage.primitives.AllPrimitivesFlat`, `idltest.datainheritancetransitive.Coupon/CouponData.Struct/MassCoupon`, `idltest.inheritance.NotiBase.Struct/NotiWithFile.Struct/NotiWithFileRevision.Struct`, `izumi.test.domain01.AllTypesObject/AllTypes.Struct/AnAdt/GoTimeImportOptional/TsuData`). **This is a real runtime defect that needs a follow-up fix** — changing `import * as moment from 'moment';` to `import moment from 'moment';` in `irt/formatter.ts` resolves it and is verified locally (the patched build runs sample_app.ts to completion against the 12 timestamp-bearing wireIds, all 213 lines emit cleanly). The defect is not introduced by R3b; it is a latent issue in the IRT runtime that the full-corpus sample app surfaces for the first time.
  - **(η) Per-class Serialized-field order requires the `serialize()` body, not the interface.** `interface TBoolNodeStructSerialized extends BoolNodeStructSerialized { tpe: TypeSerialized; lit: boolean; }` — TS extends-first interface flattening produces `[lit, tpe]` (parent's `lit` first, then own's `tpe, lit` dedup'd to just `tpe`), but the TS class's `constructor()` and `serialize()` both access `data.tpe` first then `data.lit` (matching the Scala case class declaration order `(tpe, lit)`). R3b extracts the canonical access/JSON order from each class's `serialize()` method body (the `return { tpe: this.tpe.serialize(), lit: this.lit };` literal), not the interface-extends-flattening. Without this override, positional Scala args land at the wrong fields.
  Verification: `idlc :typescript --root <main-tests> --source <main-tests>/source -t /tmp/r3b-ts-out -d withRuntime=true -d layout=PLAIN` → 329 .ts files; staged `/tmp/r3b-build/` with `regression-harness/templates/typescript/{package.json,tsconfig.json}` and sample_app.ts; `npm install --no-audit --no-fund` (26 packages) then `./node_modules/.bin/tsx sample_app.ts` → **exit 0, 201 sorted NDJSON lines**. `./node_modules/.bin/tsc --noEmit` against the staged tree reports 0 errors in `sample_app.ts` itself; the 8 pre-existing `irt/formatter.ts` "expression is not callable" errors are the F13 defect, not in scope of R3b. Sample line: `idltest.algebraics.AdtTestID	default	"AdtTestID#s1"` (identifier toString form). Lines that overlap with R3a's NDJSON are byte-identical where the TS encoder and Scala Circe produce the same JSON (verified on `coverage.crossdom.leaf.LeafDto`, `coverage.generics.blob.BlobContainers`, `coverage.generics.nested.NestedGenerics`, etc.). Lines that diverge fall under Layer-C exclusions per F10/F13. Path to R3c (C#): the C# translator (per existing `selftest-corpus/dtofields-only/.idl-regression/sample_app.cs`) DOES emit positional constructors (`new IntPairStruct(1, 2)` style), so R3c can follow the R3a Scala-style positional-args approach more closely. The TS-specific issues α/β/γ/δ/ε/ζ/η above do not apply to C#. C#-specific surprises documented in the R2 COVERAGE.md (e.g. `set[opt[<primitive>]]` skip) are already accounted for. R3c can re-use the qualified field-resolver pattern from R3b's generator, but should not need the polymorphic-field skip — C# generics + nullables map cleanly to .NET types without TS's polymorphic-dict wrapping.

- **R3a: full-corpus Scala sample app for regression-harness** (2026-05-14) — Authored `idealingua-v1/idealingua-v1-test-defs/src/main/resources/defs/main-tests/.idl-regression/sample_app.scala` (1592 LOC) covering every user-declared type the `:scala` translator emits over the full `main-tests/` corpus (39 `.domain` files + 6 `.model` files across `coverage/`, `idltest/`, `izumi/test/`, `overlaytest/`). Counts: 356 `final case class` declarations (DTOs, identifiers, struct mirrors for mixins, service- and buzzer-input/output ephemerals, ADT-branch wrappers) + 15 enums + 29 ADTs → 393 NDJSON fixture lines (one per case class + one per enum element + one per ADT branch). Construction is POSITIONAL `new <FQ>(...)` everywhere — the regression-gate the brief locked: a future field-order regression in the translator shows up as a scala-cli `--old` compile error against the committed sample app. Determinism: fixed scalars (`"s1"`, `1`, `2L`, `true`, `(1: Byte)`, etc.), fixed UUID `3a7f0c12-1234-5678-9abc-fedcba987654`, fixed timestamps anchored at `2025-01-15T10:30:45Z`, fixed TBLOB `Base64.decode("aGkh")` → `[104,105,33]`. Encoding via `(v: <wireId>).asJson.printWith(noSpaces)`; lines sorted by `(wireId, scenario)` before stdout. Companion meta: `sample_app.scala.meta` carries `idl_sha256=c4c80705ac780e3b782e41350624e9a19ed3936c8613fdebf9a37c7f37593511` per `Harness.scala:hashIdlTree` (KEY=VALUE form, not the JSON form the brief described — the existing `selftest-corpus/` samples and `SampleAppGen.scala:79-89` parser both expect KEY=VALUE; the brief was wrong about JSON, on-disk format wins). Generator: a throwaway Python script (NOT committed; the algorithm summary lives here for R3b/R3c portage). Walks the `idlc :scala` output (239 `.scala` files under `idealingua-v1-test-harness/target/generated-sources/test-harness/scala/`), extracts every `final case class` head + field list, qualifies every field type with `_root_.<pkg>.<name>` against a global name→FQ index, dispatches enums to first element, ADTs to first branch (recursively), trait fields to `<TraitFQ>.Struct` (mixins generate `<Trait>.Struct` companion case classes), package-object aliases through a resolved-alias chain (`type X = Y` in `package-object.scala`, qualified at parse-time), with a depth-4 cycle guard for self-referential types (`AllTypes`, `AppNode`, `NestedClass`). Field types in generated case classes are bare-name (e.g. `chained: LeafAliasChain`) — qualifier must same-package-prefer when multiple FQs match (e.g. `Singleton` collides between `coverage.usertypes.idsingle` and `coverage.usertypes.enumsingle`). The parser identifies `final case class` declarations via a depth-aware brace-stack scan that pushes only on `object NAME extends … {`-with-body (distinguishing empty `object X` companions from body-bearing ones via "blank-line-or-top-level-keyword-before-`{`" heuristic), and detects ADT branches by matching the case class's `extends <Parent>` clause against the in-scope sealed-trait `extends izumi.idealingua.runtime.model.IDLAdtElement` declarations (bare or dotted). Output `_root_.`-prefixes every wireId reference so the runtime jar's `izumi.idealingua.*` doesn't shadow corpus `izumi.test.*` packages. For R3b (TypeScript), the analogous algorithm walks the `:typescript` translator output, extracts ES2020 class/constructor signatures, qualifies via module path, and emits positional `new <Cls>(...)` invocations against the TS `irt` runtime. Verification: dropped the generated tree + sample_app + a scala-cli `project.scala` template (Scala 2.13.18, `-Xsource:3-cross`, izumi 1.4.19 deps, circe 0.14.10) into `/tmp/r3a-build2/` and ran `scala-cli run .` — clean compile (after working around scala-cli's auto-exclude of `test/` directories: scala-cli 1.10.1's input scanner skips any subdir literally named `test` regardless of context, so the staged copy renames `izumi/test/` → `izumi/testdata/` before running; **this is a latent defect in the regression-harness `ScalaAdapter` that needs a follow-up** — the corpus has `izumi/test/...` paths that the harness's `scala-cli run <workDir>` will silently skip on every Scala-leg invocation, defeating the harness for that subtree); exit 0, 393 NDJSON lines emitted in sorted order, all parsing as valid JSON via Circe. Sample line shape: `coverage.buzzers.alloutputs.AllOutputsBuzzer.AlgebraicEventOutput\tBuzzerOk\t{"BuzzerOk":{"v":"s1"}}`. Surprises: (a) **scala-cli `test/`-dir auto-exclude** documented above — the most material finding; needs a follow-up to ScalaAdapter.scala (pass `--exclude` with an empty pattern, or enumerate inputs explicitly). (b) **Generated case-class field types are unqualified bare names** (e.g. `chained: LeafAliasChain` from a foreign-package alias); resolving requires a global name→FQ index plus a follow-the-alias-chain step through `package-object.scala` `type X = Y` declarations — easy to miss for type-aliases vs case-class types. (c) **Identifier with one of its fields a recursive payload** never appears in the corpus, so the cycle guard's null-fallback path (`new <Self>(null.asInstanceOf[<Field>])`) is unexercised — kept the implementation for safety. (d) **`Singleton` namespace collision**: `coverage.usertypes.idsingle.Singleton` (Identifier) and `coverage.usertypes.enumsingle.Singleton` (Enum) exist in the same corpus; resolving short field-type `Singleton` requires the same-package preference rule, otherwise the wrong type wins by alphabetical order. (e) **`set[opt[<primitive>]]` skip** documented in R2 COVERAGE.md is C#-only; Scala is unaffected and `coverage.generics.nested.NestedGenerics` is fully exercised. (f) **`izumi.test.domain01.AllTypes` is recursive** (fields `list/another: List[AllTypes]`, `selfMap: Map[str,AllTypes]`, `option: Opt[AllTypes]`, `selfSet: Set[AllTypes]`); the depth-4 cycle guard produces ~4-level-deep nested encoded JSON before terminating with empty containers / `null` — large NDJSON line (~10KB) but valid and deterministic. R3a is SCALA-ONLY; R3b (TypeScript) and R3c (C#) are separate cycles. Path to R3b: port the same generator algorithm to walk the `idlc :typescript` output (sibling `target/generated-sources/test-harness/typescript/` after sbt regen), emit a TS sample app under `.idl-regression/sample_app.ts` against `irt` (the TS runtime imported via the harness's `irt` symlink), use TS positional constructor-call syntax `new Type(arg1, arg2, ...)`, sidecar meta as `sample_app.ts.meta` with same `idl_sha256=...` line. The scala-cli `test/`-exclusion defect does not affect R3b — TS doesn't use scala-cli.

- **R2: audit + expand test corpus for full feature coverage** (2026-05-14) — Phase A: produced a feature-by-feature coverage audit of the 28 existing `.domain` files in `idealingua-v1-test-defs/src/main/resources/defs/main-tests/source/` against the Primitive subclasses in `idealingua-v1-model/.../typer/ir/TypeDef.scala`, the Generic subclasses, the user-type kinds, the 5 `RawMethod.Output` variants, cross-domain references, and the misc surface (constants, annotations, docs, overlays). Wrote the audit to a new `idealingua-v1-test-defs/src/main/resources/defs/main-tests/COVERAGE.md` (~150 LOC). Phase B: added 10 minimal gap-filling fixtures under `source/coverage/` totaling ~150 LOC:
  - `primitives-flat.domain` — every primitive as a bare DTO field (`coverage.primitives.AllPrimitivesFlat`).
  - `generics-blob.domain` — TBLOB inside opt/list/set/map value (`coverage.generics.blob.BlobContainers`).
  - `generics-map-keys.domain` — non-string map keys i32/i64/u32/uid/enum (`coverage.generics.mapkeys.MapWithVariousKeys`).
  - `generics-nested.domain` — list[list[i32]] / map[str,list[T]] / list[map[str,i64]] / opt[list[map[str,T]]] (`coverage.generics.nested.NestedGenerics`).
  - `enum-single-member.domain` — single-member enum (`coverage.usertypes.enumsingle.Singleton`).
  - `identifier-single-field.domain` — single-field identifier (`coverage.usertypes.idsingle.Singleton`).
  - `service-all-outputs.domain` — one service covering Void / Singular(primitive) / Singular(DTO) / Struct / Algebraic / Alternative (`coverage.services.alloutputs.AllOutputsService`).
  - `buzzer-all-outputs.domain` — buzzer counterpart of the all-outputs witness (`coverage.buzzers.alloutputs.AllOutputsBuzzer`).
  - `crossdom-leaf.domain` + `crossdom-importer.domain` — cross-domain alias chain (`LeafAliasChain → LeafAlias → other#LeafDto`), cross-domain mixin inheritance (`& other#LeafMixin`), cross-domain ADT branch (`other#LeafDto as ForeignLeaf` inside `ForeignAdt`), foreign-typed field, foreign-typed map key (`other#LeafEnum`). New domains `coverage.crossdom.leaf` + `coverage.crossdom.importer`.

  Phase C: build-time source generator (`TestCodegenMain` invoked from `idealingua-v1-test-harness/Compile/sourceGenerators`) picks up all new fixtures automatically. C# `Driver.csproj` updated to exclude `Coverage/Services/Alloutputs/AllOutputsService.cs` and `Coverage/Buzzers/Alloutputs/AllOutputsBuzzer.cs` from compilation (same `IRT.Transport.Client` exclusion the existing 6 service/buzzer files already get). Existing 28 fixtures left untouched (history + downstream sample-app consumers depend on their exact wire shape).

  Translator defect surfaced: **C# translator emits a duplicate local variable declaration `string _<field>_d` for `set[opt[<primitive>]]`** (CS0128 + CS0165). Worked around by removing the offending field from `generics-nested.domain`; defect documented in COVERAGE.md "Translator defects surfaced" section. Other nested patterns (`opt[list[map[str,T]]]`, `list[list[i32]]`, `list[map[str,T]]`, `map[str,list[T]]`) all compile cleanly.

  Verification matrix (3 FROZEN contracts × 2 Scala versions = 6 green):
  - 2.13.18 `runWireFixtures`: 26 Scala + 20 TypeScript + 21 CSharp fixtures match.
  - 2.13.18 `runCrossLangInterop`: 118 verified, 10 skipped (F9 gap), 6 excluded (F10/F13).
  - 2.13.18 `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test`: 48/48 pass — includes `TyperDiagnosticsSpec.new typer accepts every domain in the corpus` (regression guard) accepting all 10 new fixtures.
  - 3.8.3 `runWireFixtures`: identical totals (26+20+21).
  - 3.8.3 `runCrossLangInterop`: identical totals.
  - 3.8.3 `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test`: 48/48 pass.

  Path to R3: with the corpus expanded, R3 ships full-corpus sample apps that exercise every wireId end-to-end (Scala + TS + C# round-trip) and re-checks against the v1.4.19 baseline. R4 then locks the regression contract by running the regression-harness adapter over the expanded corpus against the v1.4.19 reference.

- [x] **M2 — Implementation.** Full typer-modernization arc landed (IMPL-1..13). New typer + Domain-consuming translators (Scala/TS/C#) replace legacy typer + Typespace machinery. Wire format preserved byte-strict across all three languages. Final state: single-typer pipeline, no flag, no legacy.

The alias content is a fixed comment block (TypeScript does not natively support type aliases with constructor / casting semantics); the only variable parts are `i.id.name` and the rendered native type for `i.target`. Enum body is `export enum X { MEMBER = 'MEMBER', ... }`. **Extension chain omitted in M1** — the default TS extension set (`EnumHelpersExtension`, `IntrospectionExtension`) has no alias handler, and `EnumHelpersExtension.handleEnum` requires legacy `TSTContext` + `Enumeration` input. M2+ scope. **M2 landed 2026-05-12**: `DomainTSStruct` + `DomainTSIdRenderer` + `DomainTSCompositeRenderer` + `DomainTSInterfaceRenderer` (pre-extension product) under `totypescript/domain/`, byte-parity unit tests in `DomainTSIdRendererSpec` (2 cases: single-primitive id, multi-primitive sort-stable) + `DomainTSCompositeRendererSpec` (2 cases: single-field DTO, multi-field declaration-order) + `DomainTSInterfaceRendererSpec` (2 cases: single-field iface, multi-field declaration-order). Total **6 new byte-parity unit tests** (10 cumulative across M1+M2). Production-path is unchanged — `DomainTypeScriptTranslator.translate()` still delegates via Phase A re-derivation. **Architecture (touch-point resolution from M1 hand-off)**: (#1) interface impl-DTO companion name uses `DomainTSStruct.implId(iid) = DTOId(iid, "Struct")`, structurally equivalent to legacy `typespace.tools.implId`. (#2) DTO/Interface field flattening via `Domain.flattenedStructs(id)`, projected to legacy `Struct` shape via `DomainTSStruct.fromFlat` (mirrors `DomainScalaStruct.fromFlat` 1:1 — the legacy `StructuralQueriesImpl.scala:41` sort key + covariant duplicate-field dedup are language-agnostic and encode `typespace.structure.structure(...)` regardless of the consumer). (#3) `TypeScriptImports.apply(ts, ...)` keeps legacy `Typespace` threading at M2 — each renderer takes a `Typespace` parameter and looks up the legacy `TypeDef` via `ts.domain.types.find(_.id == newIR.id)`; a `DomainTSImports` shim is deferred to M6 when the production-swap is in scope. (#4) Identifier widens `IdField → Field` directly (no flattening — identifiers have no inheritance), matching legacy `typespace.structure.structure(i): PlainStruct` which for identifiers just exposes the declared id-fields. (#5) `TypeScriptTypeConverter.toNativeType(ts, ...)` keeps legacy `Typespace` threading per the M1 recommendation. (#6) `parentsInherited(i.id)` for the unique-interfaces set keeps legacy `ts.inheritance.parentsInherited(...)` — `Domain.parents` carries the same membership but no defined ordering; ordering switch to `Domain.parents` is deferred to a future F-followup once the order invariant is verified across the corpus. **Extension chain omitted in M2** — default TS extensions have no `handleIdentifier`/`handleDTO`/`handleInterface` handler, so the pre-extension product is byte-equal to post-extension for the default extension list. **LOC**: production 119 (`DomainTSStruct`) + 143 (`DomainTSIdRenderer`) + 179 (`DomainTSCompositeRenderer`) + 183 (`DomainTSInterfaceRenderer`) = 624; specs 218 + 279 + 294 = 791; total 1415 (under 1500 budget). Total **4 new byte-parity unit tests** (14 cumulative across M1+M2+M3). Production-path is unchanged — `DomainTypeScriptTranslator.translate()` still delegates via Phase A re-derivation. **Architecture**: (#1) ADT renderer mirrors legacy `renderAdt` + `renderAdtImpl` verbatim; `Typespace` threaded per-call for `ts.tools.implId` (interface impl id) + `ts.dealias` (alias resolution in `serialize`/`deserialize` payload-key type computation). (#2) `DomainTSServiceMethodProduct` extracts per-method codegen for `renderRPCMethodSignature`, `renderServiceMethodOutputSignature`, `renderRPCMethodModels`, `renderRPCClientMethod`, `renderServiceDispatcherHandler`, and the Algebraic / Alternative output paths (which delegate to the ADT renderer for nested ADT output models). (#3) `DomainTSNameMangling` exposes `methodToInputName` / `methodToOutputName` / `methodToPositiveTypeName` / `methodToNegativeTypeName` / `idToParaName` matching the legacy inline literals (`In<Method>` / `Out<Method>` / `<Method>Success` / `<Method>Failure`) — the legacy TS translator embedded these inline rather than as a helper trait, unlike Scala. (#4) Service/Buzzer renderer reads the legacy `Service` / `Buzzer` via `ts.domain.services.find(_.id == i.id)` / `ts.domain.buzzers.find(_.id == i.id)` so `TypeScriptImports.apply(ts, legacyDef, ...)` can walk the legacy typespace (same M2 plumbing approach). (#5) Service / Buzzer renderer threads `Typespace` per-call for the converter — same convention as M2. **Extensions**: `TypeScriptTranslatorExtension` has no `handleService` / `handleBuzzer` (legacy `renderService`/`renderBuzzer` never call `ext.extend`), so the service / buzzer pre-extension product equals post-extension product. `handleAdt` IS defined on `IntrospectionExtension` (default extension), so the M3 ADT byte-parity test compares against the legacy mirror with the SAME empty extension list — production-swap (M6) will need to thread the extension chain. **LOC**: production 141 (`DomainTSAdtRenderer`) + 365 (`DomainTSServiceMethodProduct`) + 45 (`DomainTSNameMangling`) + 356 (`DomainTSServiceRenderer`) = 907; specs 251 + 616 = 867; total 1774 (~18% over 1500 budget — the service spec necessarily mirrors all per-method legacy helpers verbatim for byte parity; compressing it without dropping coverage would diverge from the parity contract). The default TS extension chain (`IntrospectionExtension` + `EnumHelpersExtension`) is the wire-format-bridging analogue of the Scala M5 Circe port; M4 ports them to consume `Domain` directly, then M5/M6 flips the production path. **M4 landed 2026-05-12**: `DomainTSEnumHelpersExtension` + `DomainTSIntrospectionExtension` under `totypescript/domain/extensions/`, wired into `DomainTSContext` as `enumHelpersExtension` / `introspectionExtension`. The two extensions cover the full default TS extension surface — there are no other extensions in `TypeScriptTranslator.defaultExtensions`. Both consume only the new `Domain` IR (no `Typespace`): (#1) `DomainTSEnumHelpersExtension.handleEnum` reads only `TypeDef.Enum`. (#2) `DomainTSIntrospectionExtension` reads `Domain.flattenedStructs` for DTO field projection, `Domain.aliases` for alias-target resolution in `unwindType` (single Map lookup — Phase 3 `AliasDealiaser` pre-resolves all alias chains), and the per-TypeDef structure for the rest; `ts.tools.implId(iid)` is replaced by `DomainTSStruct.implId(iid) = DTOId(iid, "Struct")`. (#3) Legacy `IntrospectionExtension.handleIdentifier` uses the 3-arg `IdentifierProduct` constructor which drops `preamble` to its default `""`; the new extension matches that bug-for-bug for byte parity (the test fails loudly if legacy behaviour changes). Byte-parity unit tests in `DomainTSEnumHelpersExtensionSpec` (2 cases: single-member, multi-member) + `DomainTSIntrospectionExtensionSpec` (5 cases: enum, identifier, DTO, interface, ADT) — **7 new byte-parity tests, 21 cumulative across M1+M2+M3+M4**. **LOC**: production 43 (`DomainTSEnumHelpersExtension`) + 293 (`DomainTSIntrospectionExtension`) = 336; specs 67 + 211 = 278; total 614 (well under 1500 budget). Verification matrix 4 FROZEN × 2 Scala = 8 lines all green (2.13.18 + 3.8.3 each: `verifyGoldens` ok, `runWireFixtures` 25/19/20, `runCrossLangInterop` 112/10/6, `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test` 70/70 — adds the 7 new TS extension byte-parity cases to the 63 prior tests). **No IR gaps surfaced** at M4 — `Domain.flattenedStructs` + `Domain.aliases` + `DomainTSStruct.implId` cover the full extension surface. **M5/M6 covers**: production-swap of `DomainTypeScriptTranslator.translate()` — replace the Phase A `IDLTyper(parsed).perform()` re-derivation with direct `Domain` consumption via the M1-M4 renderers + extensions. The translate() body becomes: walk `Domain.members` (sorted by `TypeId.toString` for declaration-order stability), dispatch per-TypeDef to the matching renderer, fold the extension chain (`enumHelpersExtension` then `introspectionExtension`) over each product, then emit modules via `modules.toSource`. The byte-parity gate flips from source equality to `runWireFixtures` JSON equality at that point. **M5 landed 2026-05-12** (production-path swap): `DomainTypeScriptTranslator.translate()` no longer invokes the legacy `TypeScriptTranslator`. The new body walks `parsed.members` in declaration order (matches legacy `typespace.domain.types`/`services`/`buzzers` ordering — same source via `IDLTyper.perform`), dispatches each `TLDBaseType`/`TLDNewtype`/`TLDService`/`TLDBuzzer` to the matching M1-M3 renderer through `typesByName: Map[String, NewTypeDef]` (derived from `domain.userTypes`), applies the M4 extension chain inline per-type (enum: `DomainTSEnumHelpersExtension.handleEnum` then `DomainTSIntrospectionExtension.handleEnum`; identifier/DTO/interface/ADT: `DomainTSIntrospectionExtension.handle*`; alias/service/buzzer: no default extension touches them — matches legacy `TypeScriptTranslatorExtension` surface), wraps each product through `modules.toSource`, and appends a final `index.ts` re-export module that excludes aliases (matches legacy `buildIndexModule` `filterNot(_.id.isInstanceOf[AliasId])`). `Typespace` is still re-derived once per `translate()` via `IDLTyper(parsed).perform()` because the renderers (`TypeScriptImports` / `TypeScriptTypeConverter`) consume it for import / dealias plumbing that has no new-IR equivalent in scope for M5 — only the legacy translator delegation is removed. **Wire-format gate**: `runWireFixtures` TS leg (19 fixtures) green on both Scala 2.13.18 + 3.8.3; `runCrossLangInterop` TS-pairing rows (Scala→TS, TS→Scala, TS→C#, C#→TS) all 18/19 verified per row. **Source-byte gate (informational, not gating per the M3 relaxed-parity convention)**: `verifyGoldens` still passes — TS goldens did NOT need regeneration; the new path emits source-byte-equal output for every corpus domain. No M5-prep golden-regeneration commit needed. **Verification matrix 4 FROZEN × 2 Scala = 8 lines all green** (2.13.18 + 3.8.3 each: `verifyGoldens` ok, `runWireFixtures` 25/19/20, `runCrossLangInterop` 112/10/6, `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test` 70/70). **No IR gaps surfaced** at M5 — `parsed.members` order, `domain.userTypes` lookup, and the M1-M4 renderer surface cover every TS production path. **IR gap deferred**: TS renderers still consume `Typespace` for import plumbing; the new IR has the data (`Domain.imports`, `Domain.userTypes`, `Domain.flattenedStructs`, `Domain.aliases`) but `TypeScriptImports` walks the legacy typespace API specifically. Migration to a `DomainTSImports` shim is bundled with the IMPL-10/11 legacy-typer deletion work — it's a code-shape refactor, not a wire-format concern. **LOC delta**: production translator 158 (Phase A was 47; +111). service routes through `IClientTransport<C>` + `<Name>ClientGeneric<C>` + `<Name>Client`, buzzer through `IClientSocketTransport<C, D>` + `<Name>BuzzerHandlers`. `Service.methods` / `Buzzer.events` read directly off the new-IR `TypeDef.Service` / `TypeDef.Buzzer` cases (per F16, both live in `domain.userTypes`). **ServiceMethodProduct**: per-method codegen — signatures (`renderRPCMethodSignature` with forClient ↔ forServer split + `Action<T>` callbacks for client + struct-method context-first parameter for server), output models (`renderServiceMethodOutModel` / `renderBuzzerMethodOutModel` dispatching `Struct` / `Algebraic` / `Singular` / `Void` / `Alternative` with `Either<Left, Right>` for the alternative case), method bodies (`renderRPCClientMethod` Transport.Send pattern with struct/algebraic/alternative/singular/void return branches; `renderRPCDispatcherHandler` Unmarshal→Marshal pattern with return-existent vs Void branches; `renderRPCDummyMethod` virtual stub bodies), and per-method I/O DTOs (`renderServiceMethodInModel(DTOId(svc, name), SimpleStructure)` via `CSharpClass(id, st)` — no `flattenedStructs` lookup, since `SimpleStructure` has no `superclasses`). `DomainCSNameMangling` consolidates `In<M>` / `Out<M>` / `<M>Success` / `<M>Failure` / `idToParaName` (wire-format-visible literals — kept in lockstep with the legacy inline strings; service/buzzer envelope keys carry these names). **Extension chain still omitted at M3**: the default C# extension set (`JsonNetExtension`) has no `Adt` / `Service` / `Buzzer` / `Identifier(struct, ...)` overrides relevant to these renderers, so the pre-extension product is byte-equal to the post-extension product for the default extension list; raw-string templates substitute `""` for `${ext.preModelEmit(...)}` / `${ext.postModelEmit(...)}` interpolations and preserve the legacy whitespace exactly (verified by the unit tests, which mirror the legacy template at the test boundary). **LOC**: production 124 (`DomainCSAdtRenderer`) + 297 (`DomainCSServiceRenderer`) + 256 (`DomainCSServiceMethodProduct`) + 39 (`DomainCSNameMangling`) + 3-line `DomainCSContext` delta (47 total) ≈ 720 new lines of production; specs 204 (`DomainCSAdtRendererSpec`, 2 cases) + 494 (`DomainCSServiceRendererSpec`, 2 cases) = 698; **total ≈ 1418** (under the 1500 budget). **Verification matrix**: 4 FROZEN × 2 Scala = 8 lines all green (Scala 2.13.18 + 3.8.3 each: `ScalaTranslatorByteParitySpec` ok, `ScalaTyperParitySpec` ok, `TyperDiagnosticsSpec` ok, `NegativeSpec` 11/11); all 14 CS unit tests pass on each Scala version (4 M1 + 6 M2 + 4 M3). The legacy `protected` `renderAdt` / `renderService` / `renderBuzzer` methods are mirrored at the test boundary (by-inspection citations to `CSharpTranslator.scala:107-251` and `:474-563` and `:716-784`). **No IR gaps surfaced for CS-M3**: ADT alternatives read directly off `Adt.alternatives` (List preserves declaration order); service/buzzer rendering reads `Service.methods` / `Buzzer.events` directly; method-I/O structs use `SimpleStructure.fields` straight off the method signature (no flattening). **M4 next**: C# extensions — `JsonNetExtension` is the wire-format-critical equivalent of Circe + IntrospectionExtension (it threads the per-DTO/Interface/ADT `serialize` / `deserialize` JSON helpers + the `RTTI_PACKAGE` reflection metadata, both load-bearing for wire-format byte-equality); `NUnitExtension` is the test-DTO `[Test]` annotator (already auto-applied via the descriptor's `makeDomain` augmentation when `manifest.enableNUnit` is set, so no Domain-side rewiring needed). Anticipated IR-touch points: (i) `JsonNetExtension.preModelEmit(ctx, dto)` / `postModelEmit` chain reads `ts.structure.structure(d).all` and `ts.dealias(...)` — covered by `Domain.flattenedStructs` and the alias-dereference paths already exercised. (ii) The introspection-style `RTTI_FULLCLASSNAME` reflection metadata is already emitted inline by `CSharpClass.render(withRTTI = true)` in the M2 renderers — extension only adds the `[JsonObject]` / `[JsonConverter]` decorators, which are pure post-hoc emit. (iii) M5 production-swap will be the final milestone (flip `DomainCSharpTranslator.translate()` off Phase A delegation, regenerate C# goldens, run wire-format + cross-lang interop suites). **M4 landed 2026-05-12**: `DomainCSJsonNetExtension` under `tocsharp/domain/extensions/` (wire-format-critical) + 8-line `DomainCSContext` delta exposing `jsonNetExtension` handle + 8-case byte-parity unit test `DomainCSJsonNetExtensionSpec`. **Wire-format criticality**: the legacy `JsonNetExtension` is the C# leg's wire-format authority — its emitted `<Name>_JsonNetConverter` classes determine cross-language JSON byte-equality with the Scala/Circe and TS/IRT marshallers. M4 ports the full Newtonsoft.Json codegen surface (write/read for DTO struct, Identifier ToString-round-trip, Enum Helpers.From dispatch, Interface polymorphic `GetFullClassName()` envelope, ADT branch-tagged envelope with `wireId` discriminator, plus the `Either<L,R>` alternative converter). **IR substitutions vs legacy**: `ts.tools.implId(iface)` → `DomainCSStruct.implId(iface) = DTOId(iface, "Struct")`; `ts.inheritance.allParents(dto).find(implId==dto)` → `domain.parents.getOrElse(dtoId, Set.empty).find(p => DomainCSStruct.implId(p) == dtoId)` (membership equivalent; `implId` is injective in `iface` so list-vs-set ordering is immaterial); `ts.dealias(al)` → `domain.aliases.getOrElse(al, al)` (Phase 3 fully dealiases so no recursion). `CSharpType` + `CSharpField` + `CSharpClass` are reused unchanged; the renderer's flattened-struct projection (`DomainCSStruct.fromFlat`) is shared with the M2 composite/interface renderers. **TBLOB parity preserved (per Q3 lock + F5 legacy bug)**: legacy `:188, 241, 351` throw `???` at codegen on TBLOB fields/values; Service / buzzer products thread JsonNet `(name, struct)` pre/post into `DomainCSServiceMethodProduct.renderServiceMethodInModel` (legacy `:625-631`) so per-method `In<Method>` / `Out<Method>` DTOs gain their `<Name>_JsonNetConverter` companions (wire-format authority for service method I/O); Algebraic method outputs route through `DomainCSAdtRenderer.renderAdtImpl(preSplice, postSplice)` with a synthetic `TypeDef.Adt` for the JsonNet attribute target (mirrors legacy `:156-174` synthetic-Adt + `ext.preModelEmit(ctx, adt)`). Service / buzzer import lists union `importsDto ++ importsAdt` (deduped) — matches legacy `:676-693` `ext.imports(ctx, defn)` aggregate. `Typespace` is still re-derived once per `translate()` via `IDLTyper(parsed).perform()` because `CSharpImports.apply(definition, pkg)(ts)` walks the legacy typespace for the import-collection pass; migration to a `DomainCSImports` shim is bundled with the IMPL-10/11 legacy-typer deletion work (same trade-off as the TS M5 swap). **C# default typer flipped to NewTyper** in `GoldenCompile.compileAll` so `verifyGoldens` / `regenerateGoldens` exercise the new path. **C# goldens regenerated** — 9 files (`DTO1.cs` Diamonds + Domain02, `InheritedCovariant.cs`, `TestBuzzer.cs`, `TestService.cs`, `OptionalService.cs`, `ImportIdService.cs`, `NestedAdtsService.cs`, `TestAliasServ.cs`) — diff scope: field-emit order in diamond-inheritance DTOs (JSON keys preserved — wire-format-immaterial), JsonNet import ordering (`System.Linq` vs `Newtonsoft.Json.Linq` order — semantically identical, cosmetic only), one covariant interface (`InheritedCovariant.cs`) loses the legacy commented-out covariance line because the new path filters parent-defined fields out of the interface declaration. **Substantive IR gap surfaced and fixed inline**: `DomainCSStruct.fromFlat` covariant-override dedup now picks the **deepest** (largest-distance = parent) occurrence and populates `defn.variance` with the full type-narrowing chain — mirrors legacy `StructuralQueriesImpl.NonContradictive` `:80-87` but with `sorted.last` (parent) instead of `sorted.head` (child override). The legacy C# goldens predate covariant override propagation into the parent interface's flat struct; without this pick, `InheritedCovariantStruct` emitted `Field: CovariantA` (child) instead of `Field: Covariant` (parent), breaking the C# interface contract (`error CS0738: 'InheritedCovariantStruct' does not implement interface member 'WithCovariance.Field'. ... return type of 'Covariant'`) which propagates to a C# driver build failure under `runWireFixtures`. Renderer-level non-converter Typespace surfaces also ported: `ts.inheritance.parentsInherited(id)` → `DomainTSStruct.parentsInherited(domain, id)` (new helper); `ts.structure.structure(iid)` → `DomainTSStruct.structureOf(domain, iid)` (new helper); `ts.tools.implId(i)` → `DomainTSStruct.implId(i)` (existing); `ts.dealias(al)` → `DomainTSImports.dealias(domain, al)` (existing). `DomainTypeScriptTranslator.translate()` no longer instantiates `DomainTypespaceFacade` — the TS path is `Typespace`-free. Carry `domain: Domain` implicit instead of `ts: Typespace`. Legacy operation mappings: `ts.dealias(al)` → `domain.aliases.getOrElse(al, al)`; `ts(e).asInstanceOf[Enumeration]` → `domain.userTypes(e).asInstanceOf[TypeDef.Enum]`; `ts(i).asInstanceOf[{Identifier,Adt,Interface,DTO}]` → `domain.userTypes(i).asInstanceOf[TypeDef.{Identifier,Adt,Interface,Dto}]`; `ts.structure.structure(iid)` → `domain.flattenedStructs(iid)` + `DomainCSStruct.fromFlat`; `ts.tools.implId(i)` → `DomainCSStruct.implId(i)` (`DTOId(i, "Struct")`); `ts.inheritance.allParents(id)` → `domain.parents.getOrElse(id, Set.empty)`. **Cross-domain slice fix**: legacy `Typespace.structure.structure(eid)` resolves cross-domain via its index; new IR's `domain.flattenedStructs` carries only LOCAL ids (`StructuralFlattener` harvests foreign structs into BFS but stores `FlatStruct` only for local owners), so `DomainCSClass` now carries an optional `sourceStruct: Option[Struct]` and `renderSlice(iface)` falls back to filtering the calling class's source flat struct by `origin == iface` when the iface is cross-domain (sufficient because cross-domain mixin interfaces in the corpus carry no parents/concepts and BFS already enumerated every iface-contributed field with the iface as `origin`). **Strict-iface filter fix**: legacy `parentsInherited(id).filter(_ != id)` walks ONLY `&` (interface) edges, but new `Domain.parents` is broader (it's `transitiveParents` from `StructuralFlattener.scala:260` which includes `+` concept-mixin interfaces). `DomainCSInterfaceRenderer.validFields` now computes a strict-interface-edge transitive closure inline (via `userTypes.get(iface).struct.superclasses.interfaces`) so concept-mixed fields stay surfaced on the iface (`PointLike` `& Metadata + IntPair` regression). **Renderer signatures**: `DomainCS{Alias,Id,Composite,Interface,Adt}Renderer.render*` drop the `ts: Typespace` parameter entirely; `DomainCSServiceRenderer` and `DomainCSServiceMethodProduct` keep an `Option[Typespace]` carried only for forwarding to `DomainCSJsonNetExtension.post*` (which still consumes legacy `CSharpClass` + `Typespace` — Cs2 scope). **DomainCSContext** unchanged shape; `DomainCSImports` shim untouched (already Domain-based pre-Cs1). `DomainCSharpTranslator` keeps the lazy `ts: Typespace = DomainTypespaceFacade(domain, parsed)` for the JsonNet extension's `post*` splice calls only; renderer bodies no longer consume it. **Audit clean**: `grep -rn "Typespace\b" tocsharp/domain/` shows residual references only in `DomainCSJsonNetExtension.scala` (deferred to Cs2) + service renderer pass-through to JsonNet + the translator's lazy val that forwards to JsonNet — every other renderer and converter twin is `Typespace`-free. **Renderer-internal `CSharpType`/`CSharpClass`/`CSharpField` swap**: `DomainCS{Alias,Id,Composite,Interface,Adt,ServiceMethodProduct}` renderers use `DomainCSharpType` / `DomainCSClass` / `DomainCSField` exclusively for codegen; `DomainCSServiceMethodProduct.renderServiceMethodInModel(withExtensions=true)` constructs a single legacy `CSharpClass` ONLY at the JsonNet `postStruct` splice site (Cs2 will eliminate). **Tests updated**: 6 renderer spec files (`DomainCS{Alias,Id,Composite,Interface,Adt,Service}RendererSpec.scala`) updated to drop the `ts` argument from the new-renderer call site. **Verification matrix (4 FROZEN × 2 Scala = 8 lines all green):** Scala 2.13.18 + 3.8.3 each — `verifyGoldens` ok (no regeneration needed after the `TTime` nullability fix + slice cross-domain filter; byte-equal across the C# golden corpus), `runWireFixtures` 25 Scala + 19 TypeScript + 20 CSharp, `runCrossLangInterop` 112 verified / 10 skipped / 6 excluded, `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test` 99/99. **LOC delta**: 3 production twin files ~770 LOC + 8 renderer files edited ~150 lines + 6 test files ~14 lines. **IMPL-10-prep-Cs2 next** (port `DomainCSJsonNetExtension` off legacy `CSharpType`/`CSharpClass`/`CSharpField`+`Typespace`). The extension now takes `(implicit im: CSharpImports, domain: Domain)`; all `CSharpType(...)` calls swap to `DomainCSharpType(...)`, all `CSharpClass(...)` / `CSharpField(...)` swap to `DomainCSClass` / `DomainCSField`; the `ts.X` surface collapses to `domain.aliases.getOrElse(al, al)` (1 site, `writePropertyValue` + `readPropertyValue` + `propertyNeedsPrepare` + `prepareReadPropertyValue`) and `domain.parents.getOrElse(dtoId, Set.empty)` (1 site, `implIfaceOf`). `DomainCSServiceRenderer` + `DomainCSServiceMethodProduct` drop the `Option[Typespace]` plumbing entirely; `DomainCSharpTranslator` drops the lazy `ts: Typespace = DomainTypespaceFacade(...)` and no longer imports `Typespace`/`DomainTypespaceFacade`. No `import izumi.idealingua.model.typespace.Typespace`, no `implicit val _: Typespace`, no `: Typespace` parameter or field, no `new IDLTyper(...)` call survives anywhere in the three Domain branches. **Verification matrix (4 FROZEN × 2 Scala = 8 lines all green):** Scala 2.13.18 + 3.8.3 each — `verifyGoldens` ok (no regeneration needed; byte-equal across the C# golden corpus), `runWireFixtures` 25 Scala + 19 TypeScript + 20 CSharp, `runCrossLangInterop` 112 verified / 10 skipped / 6 excluded — six pair rows verified 18+ per row, `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test` 99/99. **LOC delta**: `DomainCSJsonNetExtension` 615 LOC (was 614 — ~5-line implicit threading delta against the renamed converters); `DomainCSServiceRenderer` and `DomainCSServiceMethodProduct` shed `Option[Typespace]` parameters and shrink slightly; `DomainCSharpTranslator` 158 LOC (was 165). The 5-file `typespace/structures/` subtree becomes unreachable after the latter deletion.
- [x] **PR-02 IMPL-13** — route NewTyperPipeline diagnostics through `LoadedDomain.VerificationFailed` (retire C8 throw-bridge from IMPL-6). Landed 2026-05-12. **Architectural change**: typing moves from translate-time back to load-time. `NewTyperPipeline.run` now returns `Either[Diagnostics, Domain]` instead of throwing `IDLException` on a non-empty diagnostic accumulator; `ModelResolver.makeLoaded` invokes the pipeline per resolved mesh and routes the result — `Right(domain)` → `LoadedDomain.Success(path, parsed, domain, warnings = Vector.empty)`; `Left(diags)` → `LoadedDomain.VerificationFailed(path, domain, diagnostics, warnings = Vector.empty)`. `TypespaceCompilerBaseFacade.compile` now reads `loaded.domain` directly instead of re-invoking the pipeline — the pipeline runs exactly once per domain. **LoadedDomain shape changes**: `Success` gains `domain: Domain` field. `VerificationFailed` rewritten to `(path: FSPath, domain: DomainId, diagnostics: typer.ir.Diagnostics, warnings: Vector[IDLWarning])` (previously `(path, domain: DomainId, issues: IDLDiagnostics)`; no production code constructed the old shape post-IMPL-10d). `TyperFailed` removed (was dead post-IMPL-10d). `LoadedModels.collectFailures` updated to format the new structured `typer.ir.Diagnostic`s via their case-class `toString` (each carries an `InputPosition`); `LoadedModels.collectWarnings` gains a `VerificationFailed` case so warnings still surface. **Module reorganisation**: `NewTyperPipeline` moved from `izumi.idealingua.translator.compat` (in `idealingua-v1-transpilers`) to `izumi.idealingua.typer` (in `idealingua-v1-model`) so the loader can invoke it without an inverse dependency on the transpilers module. The empty `translator/compat/` directory was removed. Imports updated in `NegativeSpec` (now relies on `HarnessCorpus.loadCorpus` throwing directly via `throwIfFailed` rather than an explicit per-domain pipeline invocation — the rejection path now flows through `VerificationFailed → collectFailures → IDLException`) and `TyperDiagnosticsSpec` (now iterates `LoadedModels.all` to capture both `Success` and `VerificationFailed` in one pass rather than relying on `loadCorpus.throwIfFailed` short-circuiting; preserves the per-domain capture-all behaviour of the old spec). **Diagnostic surfacing to CLI**: structured `typer.ir.Diagnostic`s (each with `InputPosition`) survive to the CLI via `LoadedModels.collectFailures` → `LoadedModels.throwIfFailed` → `IDLException`. The CLI message is at least as informative as the previous `IDLException("New typer diagnostics: ...")` (it now reports the originating domain id + file path on the first line and one structured diagnostic per nested line). **Verification matrix (4 FROZEN × 2 Scala = 8 lines all green)**: Scala 2.13.18 + 3.8.3 each — `verifyGoldens` ok (no regeneration needed; the new path produces byte-identical IR / renderer output to the previous translate-time path), `runWireFixtures` 25 Scala + 19 TypeScript + 20 CSharp, `runCrossLangInterop` 112 verified / 10 skipped / 6 excluded, `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test` 42/42 (NegativeSpec's 11 cases continue to reject; `TyperDiagnosticsSpec` regression guard green; the `NewTyperFeatureFlagSpec` smoke tests in `idealingua-v1-transpilersJVM/test` also pass via the new `loaded.domain` read). **LOC delta**: `LoadedDomain.scala` 49 → 51 LOC (new `domain` field + new `VerificationFailed` shape, drop dead `TyperFailed`); `LoadedModels.scala` +6 LOC (warnings case + new diagnostic formatter); `ModelResolver.scala` +12 LOC (typer invocation + Either dispatch); `NewTyperPipeline.scala` -5 LOC (drop throw, return Either) + moved package; `TypespaceCompilerBaseFacade.scala` -1 LOC (replace pipeline call with field read); spec updates ~30 LOC across two files. Net ~+50 LOC, mostly scaladoc.

Historical trail kept inline for reference. **Pre-flip context (retained for archival reference)**: re-gated 2026-05-12 by IMPL-9 STOP report (F8 cycle); the post-M6 `ScalaTyperParitySpec` was structurally weak (parseability + content-blind exerciser-divergence count); the real cross-typer regression guard is the new `ScalaTranslatorByteParitySpec` which compares per-module UTF-8 bytes between Legacy and NewTyper across the 28-domain corpus. The F8 fix (commit `PR-02 IMPL-7a.2-F8-fix`) brought service-method input/output rendering back to parity (zero-parameter regression eliminated), but the byte-parity spec still observes **167 divergent modules** (symmetric on Scala 2.13 + 3.8) from independent renderer-level F-followups: AnyVal field-name normalisation (legacy renames sole struct field to `value`; new keeps the declared name), Circe `<X>Circe` trait vs new `object` companion layout, upcast `<T>_upcast_<T>` self-targets vs new `<T>_upcast_<Parent>` parent-targets, downcast `case v: <T>.Struct` vs new `case v: <Implementor>` matching, missing `with Any` on `Defn` traits when the parent is non-AnyVal, and `clone X into Y { ... }` newtype divergence. (Defects #5 BFS field-order and #7 covariant duplicate-field emission were closed 2026-05-12 by commit `PR-02 IMPL-7a.2-Fa`; the file-count baseline stayed at 167 because every previously affected module also diverges for at least one other catalog entry, so the count metric did not move while the field-order/dedup content defects are off the catalog. Defects #1 AnyVal anonymous-field rename to `"value"` and #6 missing `with Any` mixin on companion `Defn` traits were closed 2026-05-12 by commit `PR-02 IMPL-7a.2-Fb`; the baseline stayed at 167 for the same reason — every affected module still diverges for at least one residual defect. Defects #3 upcast reflexive self-targets and #4 Circe mirror `case v: T.Struct =>` were closed 2026-05-12 by commit `PR-02 IMPL-7a.2-Fc`; baseline dropped **167 → 113** (–54 modules). Defect #2 Circe layout (interface-companion `StructCirce` trait + `Struct_upcast_*` set + AnyVal on impl + cast-up field-order) was closed 2026-05-12 by commit `PR-02 IMPL-7a.2-Fd`; baseline dropped **113 → 110** (–3 modules). PR-02 IMPL-7a.2-Ff1 (2026-05-12) landed the service-method extension chain, dropping the baseline **80 → 79** (–1 module, OptionalService).  `DomainServiceMethodProduct.inputDefn` / `outputDefn` now mirror the full legacy `CompositeRenderer.defns(_, CsMethodInput | CsMethodOutput)` extension chain: `DomainAnyvalExtension.withAnyvalForMethodStruct` (single-scalar-field wrappers), `DomainCastSimilarExtension.mkConvertersForMethodStruct` (`_cast_into_*` peer implicit objects), `DomainCastUpExtension.generateUpcastsForMethodStruct` (`_upcast_*` parent implicit objects), and `DomainCirceDerivationTranslatorExtension.emitForMethodStruct` (`<Name>Circe` trait sibling + companion-base `extends`, including the legacy `encodeUnwrapped<Name>` unwrap branch for `Singular(_)` outputs).  `DomainCompositeStructure.isInterfaceEphemeral` narrowed to only match `EphemeralOrigin.InterfaceMirror` (legacy parity with `TypespaceImpl.types.isInterfaceEphemeral`) so the `apply(defn: <Name>.Defn)` mirror constructor stays on service / buzzer method I/O DTOs.  Residual divergences in the affected service modules (`ImportIdService`, `NestedAdtsService`, `TestAliasServ`, `TestBuzzer`, `TestService`) come from two new F-followups: (i) `_cast_into_*` ordering still depends on `Domain.flattenedStructs` HashMap iteration, which differs from legacy's `ts.types.index.underlying` HashMap iteration over a different key set (legacy includes interface ephemerals + DTO ephemerals + service / buzzer ephemerals all in one map; new IR has them split across `flattenedStructs`, `members`, and `ephemeralOwner`); (ii) cross-domain `AnyVal`-eligibility predicate fails because `Domain.flattenedStructs` is per-domain — a method input field whose declared type lives in another domain cannot resolve `isComposite` locally without traversing `Domain.imports → familyIndex`.  PR-02 IMPL-7a.2-Fe1/Fe2/Fe3/Fe4 (2026-05-12) landed the prior cycle, dropping the baseline **110 → 96 → 86 → 82 → 80** across four coupled-fix commits: Fe1 registered `EphemeralOrigin.InterfaceMirror` ephemerals in `parents`/`implementingDtos` (so `<I>_downcast_extend_<I>Struct` emits correctly), swapped extension order to `sims ++ downs ++ ups` matching legacy `defaultExtensions`, threaded `DomainScalaStruct.fromFlat` through `generateUpcastsForImplStruct` (declaration-order field assignment), and removed the empty-signature early-return in `sameSignature` (so empty-fielded mixins like Covariant/Empty get peer `Struct_cast_into_*` entries); Fe2 routed `DomainCastDownExpandExtension` through `fromFlat` (multi-distance `using(...)` body ordering), preserved BFS-distance order in `qualifiedAncestors` and `structuralParents`, and added `DomainCastSimilarExtension.mkConvertersForImplStruct` on the inner mirror DTO companion (Pair1.Struct ↔ Pair2.Struct peer pairs); Fe3 routed `DomainCastSimilarExtension.mkConverters` through `fromFlat` so peer-cast bodies emit parents-first; Fe4 rewrote the same-name dedup in `fromFlat` to take `maxBy(distance)` for equal-type duplicates (mirroring legacy `NonContradictive`'s `Some(fields.head)` which reflects the legacy `superFields ++ embeddedFields ++ thisFields` parent-first encounter order).) The new spec records the current count as `KnownDivergenceBaseline = 79` (as of Ff1) so any regression upward fails immediately, and any improvement (an F-followup landing) fails with a "lower the baseline" instruction so the gate stays tight. Flipping `TyperImpl.NewTyper` as the default remains gated by `KnownDivergenceBaseline == 0` on both Scala versions. Legacy deletion (IMPL-10/11) remains additionally gated on Phase B for both TS+C# (the current Phase A delegation re-derives a legacy `Typespace` via `IDLTyper(parsed).perform()`).
