## Cross-cutting architectural notes (locked)

These are decisions or open questions that span multiple PRs. The full enumerated list (with recommendations) is in the meta-plan, §"Cross-cutting decisions". Tracked here as visible state.

User-decided 2026-05-03 in `docs/drafts/20260503-2159-questions-modernization-decisions.md`. Answers folded below.

- [x] **C1 / Q6 — Build alongside, behind feature flag.** New `izumi.idealingua.typer.phase.*` package coexists with `IDLPostTyper`, gated by a flag in `TypespaceCompilerBaseFacade`, until PR-03 harness proves byte-parity.
- [x] **C2 / Q2 — Delete `idealingua-v1-runtime-rpc-go` along with the Go transpiler.** Single atomic deletion in IMPL-8.
- [x] **C3 / Q8 — Keep cross-build (Scala 2.13 + Scala 3) everywhere.** **Collision with PR-02 D14 fix**: `Fingerprint(value: IArray[Byte])` is Scala 3 only. Implementation must substitute either `scodec.bits.ByteVector` or a hand-written wrapper with `equals`/`hashCode`; PR-02 §4 needs an addendum.
- [x] **C4 / Q12 — Lock `wireId = "<pkg>.<name>"` formula permanently.** No type may change package or name without a coordinated wire-format break across all consumers. Codify in `docs/wire-format.md` (PR-03 §4 spec doc).
- [x] **C5 / Q1 — Buzzers used in production; Streams not used.** New IR treats Buzzers first-class on par with Services. Streams: deprecate-but-keep-working in the new IR (no behavior change, no fixtures), schedule removal for a later release.
- [x] **C6 / Q10 — Constants in scope.** New typer adds a `ConstValueTyper` phase; closes the TODOs at `IDLPostTyper.translateValue:240,245,250`.
- [x] **C7 / Q11 — Drop `ForeignType` from the grammar; finish newtype support for all type kinds.** Existing models that use `ForeignType` get a typer diagnostic and stop compiling.
- [x] **C8 / L1 — New typer emits diagnostics, never throws on user errors.** Locked.
- [x] **C9 / Q9 — Hard remove of unused CLI flags on the M2 release.** No deprecation cycle. Deviation from recommendation; user accepts the risk that downstream automations may pass legacy flags.
- [x] **C10 / Q7 — New `idealingua-v1-test-harness` module.** Separates TS/dotnet test-time deps from the compiler module.
- [x] **C11 / L2 — Cross-domain reference graph cycles enforced before dependency-graph construction.** Locked.
- [x] **C12 / L3 — Field-ordering invariant in the new IR.** Locked. New IR preserves struct field declaration order (Circe `deriveEncoder` emits keys in declaration order; current consumers may rely on byte equality).
- [x] **Q3 — TBLOB lock base64-string for all three languages.** PR-02 implements C# in IMPL-N; PR-03 baseline must verify Scala/TS already match.
- [x] **Q4 — TUInt64 hybrid encoding.** Number for values ≤ 2^53 - 1 (JS max safe integer); string for ≥ 2^53. Boundary is documented in the wire-format spec.
- [x] **Q5 — TSet insertion order, mirrored across all three languages.** PR-02 must ensure TS/C# emitters iterate in a way that matches Scala's `LinkedHashSet`.
- [x] **L4 — Layer B byte-strict raw comparison default; canonicalization is a parallel sanity check.** Locked.
- [x] **L5 — Negative-test diagnostic-kind assertions deferred to PR-02.** PR-03 asserts only "legacy throws something". Locked.
- [x] **L6 — Freeze tag `wire-format-baseline-2026-05-03`.** Locked. Tag cut 2026-05-08 at commit `4953dac` (PR-02 IMPL-8 — Go/Protobuf deletion). All four harness contracts green at the tagged commit (`verifyGoldens`, `runWireFixtures`, `runCrossLangInterop`, `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test`). PR-02 IMPL-1+ must reproduce the harness baselines byte-strictly against this tag.
- [x] **C13 / R1 — PR-03.1 corpus scope = broader (28 `.domain` files).** Resolved 2026-05-04 by user. Scope: all `.domain` files under `idealingua-v1-test-defs/src/main/resources/defs/main-tests/source/`, comprising `idltest/` (22) + `izumi/test/` (5: clashing, clashing/another, domain01, domain02, domain03recursive01) + `overlaytest/` (1: withoverlay). Resolves master plan §11 Q9 (recommendation accepted). Master plan §12 PR-03.1 wording corrected; §3 / §5 narrower phrasings stand as descriptive of the predominant corpus and are read in light of this resolution.

### New follow-ups created by user answers

- [x] **F16 — Master plan §4 IR pseudocode mismatch with legacy `TypeId` hierarchy.** RESOLVED in PR-02 IMPL-3 (2026-05-08): IMPL-2 widened legacy `TypeId` to include `ServiceId`/`BuzzerId`/`StreamsId` with catch-alls; IMPL-3 absorbed `ServiceDef`/`BuzzerDef`/`StreamsDef` into `TypeDef.Service`/`Buzzer`/`Streams`; `Domain` + `ResolvedDomain` no longer carry separate service-family lists. IMPL-2 specs needed zero updates (they never referenced the dropped fields).
- [x] **F19 — `ResolvedDomain.consts: List[RawConst]` carry-through.** RESOLVED in PR-02 IMPL-3 (2026-05-08): Phase 8 `ConstValueTyper` converts `RawConst` to typed `Const`; `Domain.consts: List[Const]` populated by Assembler.
- [x] **F1 — Substitute Scala-3-only `IArray[Byte]` in PR-02 §4 `Fingerprint` (per C3/Q8 cross-build decision).** Resolved 2026-05-03 — picked `scodec.bits.ByteVector` as the primary type (hand-equality-wrapper noted as fallback). PR-02 §4 IR pseudo-code and the frozen-after-assembly invariant paragraph updated.
- [x] **IMPL-7a.2-F1 — Recursive ADT (`{idltest.json}`) rejected by new typer CycleDetector.** Diagnostic captured 2026-05-12 via `TyperDiagnosticsSpec` (commit `a6def18`): Phase 5 `CycleDetector` emits `CyclicUsage(JLObject → JSONLike → JLArray)` + `NonTerminatingCycle(...)` at `/idltest/jsonlike.domain:124-170`. Legacy accepts. **Triage: T1 (typer bug).** The recursion is well-founded — every cycle traversal goes through `JLArray.values: list[JSONLike]` (a container indirection that legitimately breaks the cycle for serialization). `CycleDetector.scala` does not currently descend container element types when classifying back-edges. **Fix**: in `idealingua-v1-model/src/main/scala/izumi/idealingua/typer/phase/CycleDetector.scala`, treat list/set/map element-type edges as cycle-breaking (the back-edge type carries a container indirection ⇒ terminating). Re-baseline `idltest.json` golden after fix. Prerequisite for IMPL-7b/7c/9. **Resolved 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-3-fix`: the existing container-element edges were already marked `Container`; the residual rejection was caused by ADT alternative edges being classified `Direct` (so an ADT-in-cycle was treated as a non-terminating back-edge). The fix downgrades ADT alternative edges and `DefMethod.Output.Algebraic` alternative edges to `Container` kind — selecting one alternative of a sum is structurally an indirection, matching the legacy `CyclicUsageRule` (which only reports an ADT cycle when *every* alternative is itself cyclic). `{idltest.json}` now passes the new typer; removed from `ScalaTyperParitySpec` exclusion list + `TyperDiagnosticsSpec` expectations.
- [x] **IMPL-7a.2-F2 — Covariant field overrides (`{idltest.inheritance}`) flagged by new typer StructuralFlattener.** Diagnostic captured 2026-05-12: Phase 6 `StructuralFlattener` emits `FieldNameConflict(InheritedCovariant, field, [CovariantA, Covariant])` + `FieldNameConflict(CovariantDTO2, field, [CovariantA, Covariant])` at `/idltest/inheritance.domain:403-561`. **Correction**: prior wording attributed this to Phase 12 Validator; the diagnostic kind is `FieldNameConflict`, which `Diagnostics.scala:104-106` lists under Phase 6. Legacy accepts because `InheritedCovariant` declares `field: CovariantA` overriding the parent `WithCovariance.field: Covariant` (a covariant subtype refinement). **Triage: T1 (typer bug).** `StructuralFlattener`'s field-merge rule treats two same-named fields with non-identical type-ids as a hard conflict regardless of subtype relationship. **Fix**: in `idealingua-v1-model/.../phase/StructuralFlattener.scala`, when two same-named fields have types T1 and T2, accept the merge if T1 <: T2 or T2 <: T1 (where <: is the interface-extends / mixin-includes closure built by Phase 0). Pick the most-derived type for the flattened field. **Resolved 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-3-fix: StructuralFlattener allows covariant field-type override`: `StructuralFlattener.flatten` now mirrors the legacy `StructuralQueriesImpl.NonContradictive` rule (`StructuralQueriesImpl.scala:72-91`). For a group of fields sharing a name with non-identical types, sort by BFS `distance` ascending; the closest declaration becomes the primary; the merge is accepted as a soft conflict iff every other candidate's type is in the primary type's transitive inherited closure (`parentsMap(child).contains(ancestor)`). Primitives admit no subtyping (legacy short-circuit). Three new unit tests in `StructuralFlattenerSpec.scala`: covariant override (child refines parent's field type), unrelated-types hard conflict (regression guard), diamond-inheritance covariant pattern (`InheritedCovariant`/`CovariantDTO2` shape from the fixture). `ScalaTyperParitySpec` exclusion list shrinks 3 → 2 (`{idltest.consts, idltest.services}`); `TyperDiagnosticsSpec` expectations shrink 3 → 2 in lockstep.
- [x] **IMPL-7a.2-F3 — Top-level scalar / list / map consts (`{idltest.consts}`) rejected by new typer ConstValueTyper.** Diagnostic captured 2026-05-12: Phase 8 `ConstValueTyper` emitted seven `BadConstValue(name, "top-level const must be CTyped/CTypedList/CTypedObject, got C{Int,String,Map,List}", ...)` diagnostics for `someInt = 1`, `someString = "xxx"`, `map = {…}`, `list = [1,2]`, `anothermap = {…}`, `complexlist = […]`; plus four `ConstTypeMismatch(typedlist|typedlist1|insanelist, TestPair, CMap)` diagnostics. The second axis turned out to be NOT "list literal mis-classified as CMap" but **untyped object literal (CMap) used as a list element against a `StructureId` target**: each `{ name="test", value=265 }` parses as `RawVal.CMap` (no inline `TypeId` annotation) and the typer's recursion into `lst[TestPair]` elements had no arm for `(StructureId, CMap)`. Plus Phase 2 `UnknownTypeRef(.XXX)` from the deliberate-typo `anotherString: XXX = """yyy"""` line — legitimate. **Legacy "accepts" the whole file** only because legacy does not process top-level consts at all: `IDLPretyper.perform` collects them into `DomainMeshLoaded.consts`, but no downstream phase reads them (`IDLPostTyper.translateValue` at `IDLTyper.scala:216-253` is only ever called for annotation values, not consts; lines 52-54 even carry a TODO log comment "Constants aren't supported yet"). The new typer's `UnknownTypeRef(.XXX)` is strictly more correct. **Resolved 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-3-fix: ConstValueTyper top-level untyped + list-literal routing`: (a) `typeCheckTopLevel`'s catch-all arm now translates untyped scalars/lists/maps via `translateRawUntyped` and returns `(value, true)` instead of emitting `BadConstValue` + sentinel — mirrors legacy's no-op acceptance for top-level untyped consts. (b) `typeCheckValue` gains an arm for `(sid: StructureId, RawVal.CMap(kvs))` that routes the CMap through `typeCheckObject` (same path as `CTypedObject`) and wraps as `ConstValue.CTypedObject` — closes the `lst[TestPair]` element-recursion gap. 8 new unit tests in `ConstValueTyperSpec.scala` (int/str/float/bool top-level untyped; list/map top-level untyped; CMap-as-struct-literal inside `lst[StructureId]`; regression-guard: int literal against `lst[str]` still fails). The 11 F3 diagnostics on `idltest.consts` are gone; only the legitimate `UnknownTypeRef(.XXX)` remains. `idltest.consts` stays excluded from `ScalaTyperParitySpec` under **new F-followup IMPL-7a.2-F5e** (legacy silently ignores the typo; new typer surfaces it); `TyperDiagnosticsSpec` expectation flipped `BadConstValue → UnknownTypeRef`.

Legacy "accepts" because legacy never processes top-level consts (`IDLTyper.scala:44-54`); new typer's resolver legitimately flags the unresolved type-ref. **Triage: T3 (user-decision / fixture defect).** **Resolved 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-3/5-fix: F5e typo + F5c synthesized-ADT validator relaxation`: chose path (a) — fixture typo corrected (`anotherString: XXX = """yyy"""` → `anotherString: str = """yyy"""`). The fixture predates the new typer and the unintentional typo is now spelled out as `str`, matching the assigned value `"yyy"`. `verifyGoldens` stayed green after the change (no golden regeneration needed) — confirming the legacy diagnosis that top-level consts are never reflected in legacy codegen output. `idltest.consts` removed from `ScalaTyperParitySpec.excludedDomainIds` and from `TyperDiagnosticsSpec.expectations`.
- [x] **IMPL-7a.2-F4 — Cross-sub-domain type references (`{izumi.test.clashing}`) rejected by new typer NameResolver.** Diagnostic captured 2026-05-12: Phase 2 `NameResolver` emits `UnknownTypeRef(izumi.test.clashing.another.SomeEnum)` at `/izumi/test/clashing.domain:64-117` (the `alias Imported = izumi.test.clashing.another#SomeEnum` line). The sub-domain `izumi.test.clashing.another` is loaded successfully (it appears in the corpus and is imported via `import izumi.test.clashing.another`); legacy resolves the cross-domain `#`-qualified reference; new typer's resolver scope does not honour transitive imports from the family index. **Triage: T1 (typer bug).** **Fix**: in `idealingua-v1-model/.../phase/NameResolver.scala`, when resolving a fully-qualified `domain.path.Type` reference, consult `IdealinguaFamilyManager`'s loaded `FamilyIndex` (already constructed in Phase 0) for matching domain ids and pull `ScopeBuilder` output for those imported domains into the resolution scope. Also covers a subset of F5a (idltest.aliases) and F5b (izumi.test.domain02) below. **Resolved 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-2/3-fix`: `NameResolver.resolveRef` now consults `FamilyIndex.domains` for cross-domain `#`-qualified references; the family is threaded through `NewTyperPipeline.run` and the new `NameResolver(scoped, family)` arity (the single-arg overload is kept for tests).
- [x] **IMPL-7a.2-F5a — Cross-domain `#`-qualified alias targets (`{idltest.aliases}`) rejected by new typer NameResolver.** Diagnostic captured 2026-05-12: Phase 2 `NameResolver` emits `UnknownTypeRef(idltest.aliases2.M2)` on `alias A2 = idltest.aliases2#M2` (`/idltest/aliases.domain:87-117`), which then cascades into Phase 6 `MissingMixin(D1, A1)` + `MissingMixin(D1, A2)` because `A1`/`A2` are no longer registered as mixin targets. Legacy accepts. **Triage: T1 (typer bug).** **Root cause: identical to F4** — `NameResolver` does not honour cross-domain imports from `FamilyIndex`. F4 + F5a should be fixed by a single resolver patch. The cascading `MissingMixin` is a downstream artefact and disappears once `A1`/`A2` resolve. **Resolved 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-2/3-fix`: the F4 resolver patch fixed `A2`; in addition `NameResolver.toStruct` now resolves `RawStructure.interfaces` (parser-produced `InterfaceId(undefined, name)` references) through the same name-resolution machinery and dealiases one alias-hop so `& A1`/`& A2` mix references find their underlying mixin (matches the legacy `fixSimpleId` alias-arm at `IDLTyper.scala:549-561`); and `StructuralFlattener` skips the `MissingMixin` check for cross-domain supertypes (foreign domains validate their own types — matches the legacy `getDomain(...).fixSimpleId` recursive dispatch at `IDLTyper.scala:567`).
- [x] **IMPL-7a.2-F5b — Selective-import + identity-alias (`{izumi.test.domain02}`) rejected by new typer NameResolver + AliasDealiaser.** Diagnostic captured 2026-05-12: Phase 2 `NameResolver` emits five `UnknownTypeRef`s on names listed in `import izumi.test.domain01.{ RTestEnum TestValIdentifier as TVI ImportAppId GenericFailure GenericFailureData }` and used with their cross-domain `#`-qualified form (e.g. `izumi.test.domain01#BasicFailure`, `izumi.test.domain01#TestValIdentifier`, `izumi.test.domain01#IDForDomain2`, `izumi.test.domain01#TestObject`, `izumi.test.domain01#GoAliasEnumTest`). Plus Phase 3 `AliasDealiaser` emits `CyclicAlias([AnyValTest2, AnyValTest2])` + `CyclicAlias([RTestObject1, RTestObject1])` on identity aliases `alias RTestObject1 = izumi.test.domain01#RTestObject1` and `alias AnyValTest2 = izumi.test.domain01#AnyValTest2` — the dealiaser confuses `local#A` with `other#A` when their simple names collide. Legacy accepts. **Triage: T1 (typer bug, two related defects).** **Fix**: (a) same resolver patch as F4/F5a covers the five `UnknownTypeRef`s; (b) `AliasDealiaser.scala` must use fully-qualified `TypeId`s when building the alias graph, not bare simple names, so `{local}/#A` and `{other}/#A` are distinct nodes. **Resolved 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-2/3-fix`: (a) the F4 resolver patch fixed all five `UnknownTypeRef`s; (b) the apparent `CyclicAlias([X, X])` was actually a downstream symptom of (a) — the resolver was returning the local alias's own `AliasId` as its target (because `localNames.get(name)` fell through to the local alias when no cross-domain lookup existed), producing a true self-loop edge `domain02.X → domain02.X`; once the resolver consults the family for qualified refs the target becomes the foreign `DTOId(domain01.X)` and the cycle disappears. The `AliasDealiaser` already keys its visited set by `AliasId` (which carries `TypePath.domain`), so no code change was needed there — the new spec test `identity alias to same-name in another domain is not cyclic` codifies this.
- [x] **IMPL-7a.2-F5c — Service alternative-output ADT synthesis (`{idltest.services}`) rejected by new typer Validator.** Diagnostic captured 2026-05-12: Phase 12 `Validator` emits four `PrimitiveAdtMember(TestService#AlternativeGeneric2Output, #map)` / `#lst` / `#set` (positions `Undefined`, indicating synthesized types) on `def alternativeGeneric() => list[SuccessData] !! set[ErrorData]`, `def alternativeGeneric2() => map[str,SuccessData] !! map[string, ErrorData]`. Plus `DuplicateAdtBranch(TestService#AlternativeSameOutput, SuccessData)` on `def alternativeSame(...) => SuccessData !! SuccessData`. Legacy accepts. **Triage: T1 (typer bug) for the `PrimitiveAdtMember` diagnostics, T2/T3 (user-decision) for `DuplicateAdtBranch`.** (T1) Phase 7 `EphemeralSynthesizer` synthesizes ADT outputs for `X !! Y` method shapes; when X or Y is a builtin container like `list[T]`/`map[K,V]`, the synthesized ADT carries a primitive branch. Either (a) wrap the container in an auto-generated DTO so the ADT branch resolves to a user type, OR (b) relax `PrimitiveAdtMember` for synthesized alternative-output ADTs. Legacy goldens already encode option (a) — check `golden/scala/idltest/services/TestService.scala` for the synthesized wrapper class. (T2/T3) `SuccessData !! SuccessData` produces an ADT with two identical branches; legacy clearly tolerates this (golden exists). New typer's `DuplicateAdtBranch` rule is stricter. **User decision needed**: keep stricter rule and require the fixture to use distinct branches, OR special-case the rule for synthesized alternative-output ADTs. **T1 RESOLVED 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-3-fix: EphemeralSynthesizer auto-wraps non-DTO alt-output branches`: `EphemeralSynthesizer.synthesizeAltBranch` now auto-wraps `Output.Singular` branches whose `typeId` is a `Builtin` (Primitive or `Generic.{TList,TSet,TMap,TOption}`) in a synthesized DTO `<MethodBase><Success|Failure>` with a single `value: <branchTypeId>` field; the outer ADT's `AdtMember.typeId` points at the wrapper DTO instead of the bare Builtin. Non-Builtin branches (DTO/Interface/Identifier/Enum/Adt) pass through unchanged. Byte parity preserved: legacy Scala renderer (used by IMPL-7a.2 Phase A) re-derives its own Typespace from the parsed AST and ignores new-typer IR, so the wrap is invisible to codegen. `TyperDiagnosticsSpec` capture for `{idltest.services}` shrinks from 5 diagnostics (4 `PrimitiveAdtMember` + 1 `DuplicateAdtBranch`) to 1 (only `DuplicateAdtBranch`). **T2/T3 RESOLVED 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-3/5-fix: F5e typo + F5c synthesized-ADT validator relaxation`: chose the "special-case the rule for synthesized alternative-output ADTs" path. `AdtConflictsRule.apply` in `idealingua-v1-model/.../typer/phase/rules/AdtConflictsRule.scala` now guards each ADT branch with `case a: TypeDef.Adt if !domain.ephemeralOwner.contains(a.id)` — user-declared ADTs (those *not* keyed in `Domain.ephemeralOwner`) continue to be validated; synthesized ADTs (Phase 7 `EphemeralSynthesizer` outputs for service alt-outputs) are exempt. This is the cleanest invariant-level discrimination: `ephemeralOwner` already maps every synthesized type back to its owner, so no new flag is needed on `TypeDef.Adt`. Header scaladoc updated to record the rationale and link back to F5c T2/T3. `idltest.services` removed from `ScalaTyperParitySpec.excludedDomainIds` and from `TyperDiagnosticsSpec.expectations`.
- [x] **IMPL-7a.2-F5d — Recursive ADT through interface members (`{idltest.ast}`) rejected by new typer CycleDetector.** Diagnostic captured 2026-05-12: Phase 5 `CycleDetector` emits `CyclicUsage(LamNode → AppNode → AST → IfNode)` + `NonTerminatingCycle(...)` at `/idltest/ast.domain:424-482`. The cycle goes through `AppNode.fun: opt[AST]` / `LamNode.body: opt[AST]` / `IfNode.cond: opt[AST]` — all `opt[]`-wrapped (container indirection). Legacy accepts. **Triage: T1 (typer bug).** **Same root cause as F1** — `CycleDetector` does not treat `opt[T]` (and by extension `list[T]`/`set[T]`/`map[K,V]`) as cycle-breaking. F1 + F5d should be fixed by a single `CycleDetector` patch (recognize container types as terminating back-edges). **Resolved 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-3-fix`: same patch as F1 — ADT alternative edges downgraded from `Direct` to `Container`. `{idltest.ast}` now passes the new typer.
- [x] **IMPL-7a.2-F7 — Same-domain `TypeId.path.domain == DomainId.Undefined` divergence from legacy `IDLTyper`.** Surfaced 2026-05-12 during IMPL-7a.2 Phase B M2 corpus-wide exerciser (commit `4bb48cb` worked around by matching legacy↔new IR by simple name): the new typer's `TypeDef.id` and the `TypeId`s returned from `NameResolver.resolveRef` for unqualified same-domain references carried `TypePath.domain == DomainId.Undefined` instead of the owning `DomainId`. Manifests downstream as same-domain alias targets rendered as `` `.`.M0 `` instead of legacy's unqualified `M0`, and as renderer divergences in 12 of 28 corpus domains (`idltest.clones`, `izumi.test.clashing.another`, `izumi.test.domain03`, `izumi.test.domain01`, `izumi.test.clashing`, `idltest.aliases`, `idltest.enums`, `idltest.identifiers`, `izumi.test.domain02`, `idltest.events`, `idltest.services`, `idltest.dtofields`). Legacy parity is byte-equal only because the production `DomainScalaTranslator` re-derives a legacy `DomainDefinition` from the raw AST and delegates to the legacy `ScalaTranslator` (IMPL-7a.2 Phase A) — the divergence is invisible to byte-output but blocks the Phase B+ direct-IR swap. **Root cause:** the parser (`ParsedId.typePath`) emits `DomainId.Undefined` for any locally declared identifier (no qualifying package). Legacy `IDLPostTyper.fixPkg` / `fixServiceId` / `fixBuzzerId` / `fixStreamsId` rewrites this to the owning `DomainId` immediately; the new typer's `ScopeBuilder` and `NameResolver` did not perform the same rewrite, so every locally declared `TypeId` and every local lookup result carried `DomainId.Undefined`. **Resolved 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-2-fix: TypeId normalization for same-domain references`: (a) `ScopeBuilder.scala` gained a private `normalize(tid, rootId)` helper (mirrors legacy `fixPkg` for structural ids and `fixServiceId`/`fixBuzzerId`/`fixStreamsId` for service-family ids); it is applied at registration time when populating `localNames` / `index` (both for the root domain and for imported domains via `collectLocalNames`). A package-private `normalizeId` entry point is exposed for cross-phase reuse. (b) `NameResolver.scala`'s per-domain `Ctx` gained a private `own(t)` wrapper that calls `ScopeBuilder.normalizeId(t, scoped.domainId)`; every `fix*` method that consumes `d.id` from the raw AST now wraps the id through `own(...)` before threading it into the IR `TypeDef.*`. `collectExportedNames` also normalises its returned ids so cross-domain `familyScope` lookups carry the foreign domain's owning `DomainId`. (c) `DomainScalaTranslator.exerciseAliasAndEnumRenderers` reverted from simple-name matching to direct id-keyed `Map[TypeId, LegacyTypeDef.*]` lookup (the previous fallback was only needed because the IR-shape diverged). `None` results from id-keyed lookup remain silent skips to tolerate orthogonal IR-shape divergences (e.g. `clone M0 into M2 { ... }` becomes a `TypeDef.Alias` in the new IR but a DTO/Interface extension in legacy — separate F-followup, out of scope). (d) `ScalaTyperParitySpec` now also asserts `DomainScalaTranslator.rendererDivergences.isEmpty` after the corpus run so any future regression of the TypeId-normalization invariant fails the parity gate.

  **Fix order (recommended).** Group by shared root cause to maximise repair-density per patch:

  1. **NameResolver / AliasDealiaser patch** → fixes F4 + F5a + F5b (3 of 8 domains). Single phase (Phase 2 + Phase 3); largest blast radius; unblocks every cross-domain test path. Should be first.
  2. **CycleDetector container-indirection patch** → fixes F1 + F5d (2 of 8 domains). Single phase (Phase 5); independent of #1; can be done in parallel.
  3. **StructuralFlattener covariant-field-merge patch** → fixes F2 (1 of 8 domains). Single phase (Phase 6); independent.
  4. **ConstValueTyper top-level-and-list-typing patch** → fixes F3 (1 of 8 domains). Single phase (Phase 8); independent.
  5. **EphemeralSynthesizer / Validator alternative-output patch** → fixes F5c (1 of 8 domains). Two phases (Phase 7 + Phase 12); **blocked on user decision for the `DuplicateAdtBranch` half** (see F5c T2/T3 note).

  After fixes 1-4 (4 patches across 4 phases), 7 of 8 domains should be accepted and `ScalaTyperParitySpec`'s exclusion list shrinks to `{idltest.services}` until the user decides F5c.

Remaining: F2 (StructuralFlattener covariant), F3 (ConstValueTyper top-level), F5c (Validator alternative-output ADTs).

Tracked as new F-followup IMPL-7a.2-F5e. `TyperDiagnosticsSpec` expectation flipped from `BadConstValue` to `UnknownTypeRef`. Remaining (typer-bug T1): F5c (Validator alternative-output ADTs). - [x] **IMPL-7a.2-F8 — Ephemeral input/output DTOs missing from `Domain.flattenedStructs`.** Surfaced 2026-05-12 by the IMPL-9 STOP report: `EphemeralSynthesizer` (Phase 7) inserts every method input/output as `Member.Ephemeral(EphemeralDto)` in `rd.members`, but `StructuralFlattener` (Phase 6) ran BEFORE the synthesizer and only consulted `rd.userTypes`, so no ephemeral DTO ever received a `FlatStruct` entry. Downstream `DomainServiceMethodProduct.scala:200-203` reads `ctx.domain.flattenedStructs.getOrElse(typespaceId, FlatStruct(typespaceId, Nil, Nil, Nil))` — the fallback returned an empty field list and every service method under `--typer=new` rendered with **zero parameters**. The earlier `ScalaTyperParitySpec` parseability-only smoke test (post-M6) did not detect this because zero-parameter Scala is still parseable; the corpus exerciser was content-blind. **Resolved 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-7a.2-F8-fix`: (a) `StructuralFlattener` collects flattenable structs from BOTH `rd.userTypes` (user-declared DTOs + Interfaces) AND `rd.members.collect{ case Member.Ephemeral(...) }` (synthesized ephemerals) into a single `views: Map[StructureId, StructView]` and BFS-flattens every entry in one pass. The `parents` / `implementingDtos` maps continue to cover only user-declared structural types so renderer queries that read "user types only" do not see synthesized mirrors. (b) The pipeline order in `NewTyperPipeline.run` swapped from `EphemeralSynthesizer(StructuralFlattener(withCycles))` to `StructuralFlattener(EphemeralSynthesizer(withCycles))` so the flattener sees the freshly synthesized ephemerals. `EphemeralSynthesizer` does not depend on `flattenedStructs`/`parents` so the swap is safe. (c) New regression spec `ScalaTranslatorByteParitySpec.scala` under `idealingua-v1-test-harness/.../harness/`: compiles every domain in the 28-corpus under both `TyperImpl.Legacy` and `TyperImpl.NewTyper`, computes per-module UTF-8 byte equality, and asserts the divergence count does not exceed a recorded baseline (`KnownDivergenceBaseline`). Failing the spec upward indicates a new defect; failing it downward asks to lower the baseline so the gate stays accurate. Verification: `idealingua-v1-modelJVM/test` 84/84 on both Scala 2.13.18 and 3.8.3 (zero regressions across the 22 phase specs); `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test` 48/48 on both Scala versions (47 prior tests all green + 1 new byte-parity gate). F8-specific evidence: in the byte-parity divergence inventory the first divergent line of every service-bearing module (e.g. `OptionalService.scala` at L73 — well below the trait/method-signature region) shows the service traits + method signatures (`def optionalMethod(a, b, c)`) match byte-for-byte between Legacy and NewTyper. **Remaining gap**: `KnownDivergenceBaseline = 167` modules diverge for unrelated F-followups (AnyVal field-name renaming to `value`, Circe `XCirce` trait vs new `object` companion layout, upcast `T_upcast_T` self-targets, downcast `case v: T.Struct` vs implementor matching, BFS field-order vs legacy depth-first, missing `with Any` on `Defn` traits) — symmetric on Scala 2.13 and 3.8. These are documented in the new spec's `KnownDivergenceBaseline` comment and become individual F-followups against the IMPL-9 gate. Once the baseline reaches `0` IMPL-9 can flip the default safely.

- [x] **F2 — TBLOB cross-language verification pre-baseline.** PR-03.1 (baseline capture) must confirm Scala and TS already encode `TBLOB` as base64-string before the freeze tag is cut; if they don't, Q3 reopens. **Resolved 2026-05-12** alongside F5: the corpus now includes `idltest.blobtest.BlobHolder` with a TBLOB field. All three emitters encode TBLOB as base64 string (standard alphabet, padded). Cross-language interop matrix (`runCrossLangInterop`) verifies the round-trip across all six Scala/TS/C# pairs.
- [ ] **F9 — Service/Buzzer input class export status in TypeScript transpiler (surfaced during PR-03.3a planning).** Legacy TS transpiler emits service/buzzer method input classes WITHOUT `export` (e.g. `class InSimple`, `class InEmpty` in `golden/typescript/idltest/services/TestService.ts:250` and `golden/typescript/idltest/events/TestBuzzer.ts:24`). Only `Out*` classes are exported. PR-03.3a cannot fixture these in TS (5 wireIds excluded — PR-03.2 §5 rows 16-20). C# may have the same issue (verify in PR-03.3b). New typer (PR-02) should consider exporting input classes to enable cross-language Layer C coverage of service-method I/O.
- [~] **F10 — Cross-language byte-stability matrix (PR-03.4 verifier; final resolution awaits PR-02 typer rewrite).** Status flipped from `[ ]` to `[~]` post-PR-03.4: Layer C now exercises and documents the divergences via `runCrossLangInterop`'s exclusion set. 6 tuples excluded, all language-emitter divergences (Optional-None TS/Scala asymmetry, AllTypes.Struct unsigned-int wrap, int64-2^53 precision). Final `[x]` requires the new typer to produce uniform behavior across emitters. PR-03.3a + PR-03.3b documented multiple legitimate per-language divergences. Scala vs TS: Optional-None (`null` vs key-drop), float 0.0 (`0.0` vs `0`), unsigned wrap (signed-wrap vs throws-on-negative), int64 ≥ 2^53 (full-range vs 2^53-truncate), time UTC (literal `Z` vs `+00:00`). C# vs Scala/TS: identifier URL-escaping case (C# lowercase `%3a` vs Scala/TS uppercase `%3A`); `ts` zoned UTC (C# matches TS `+00:00`, both diverge from Scala `Z`); `tsuni` (C# matches Scala literal `Z`). Layer C (PR-03.4) needs to verify cross-encode parity at the DECODED level (e.g. Scala-encoded `null`-as-Option round-trips correctly through TS-decoder), not at byte level. Each documented divergence becomes a Layer C row.
- [ ] **F11 — `moment` library dependency in IRT runtime (long-running concern).** `idealingua-v1-runtime-rpc-typescript/.../formatter.ts:2` imports `moment`, in maintenance mode. PR-02 should evaluate replacing with `dayjs` or native `Intl.DateTimeFormat`. PR-03.3a pins `moment` ^2.29.4 in the harness's TS package.json; future runtime upgrade requires re-baselining time fixtures.
- [ ] **F14 — NuGet provisioning in airgapped CI (analog of F12 for the C# leg).** PR-03.3b harness invokes `dotnet build` with default NuGet source resolution; airgapped CI needs primed `~/.nuget/packages/` or a local NuGet feed. Document CI provisioning requirement when CI lands.
- [ ] **F17 — `scodec-bits` JS variants not in `deps.lock.json` (airgapped CI concern; analog of F12/F14).** PR-02 IMPL-1 added `scodec-bits` 1.1.38 to model module's dependencies. JVM variants (`_2.13`, `_3`) were already transitively present in `deps.lock.json`; JS variants (`sjs1_2.13`, `sjs1_3`) need a Nix flake refresh to be captured for airgapped CI. Out of IMPL-1 scope.
- [ ] **F15 — Per-leg `FailureKind` enum duplication (cosmetic cleanup).** PR-03.3a's `WireFixtureTypescriptRunner.FailureKind` and PR-03.3b's `WireFixtureCSharpRunner.FailureKind` copy the same 6-case enum. Deduplicate to a shared trait in a future cleanup PR. No correctness impact.
- [ ] **F13 — `izumi.test.domain01.AllTypes.Struct` TS-leg fixture dropped — moment import incompatibility under tsx runtime (surfaced during PR-03.3a T3).** TS goldens import `moment` via `import * as moment from 'moment'` (legacy CommonJS interop). Under tsx 4.21 + Node.js 24, esbuild's CJS transform converts this to `var moment = __toESM(require("moment"))`, where `__toESM` wraps in a non-callable Object.create(Function.prototype). The IRT `Formatter.readZoneDateTime` calls `moment(d)` and throws `TypeError: moment is not a function`. tsc passes (compile-time types resolve correctly with `esModuleInterop: false`); tsx fails at runtime. Three potential fixes: (a) modify `idealingua-v1-runtime-rpc-typescript/.../irt/formatter.ts` to use `import moment from 'moment'` (default import) AND flip `esModuleInterop: true` in harness tsconfig — runtime-module change with downstream-consumer impact; (b) switch harness from tsx to `tsc → node dist/`, removing the esbuild transform layer; (c) accept the drop for now. PR-03.3a chose (c). 16 of 17 wireIds and 19 fixtures land. PR-03.4 should re-evaluate when Layer C cross-language coverage matters more. AllTypes wire-format primitives are partially covered by other fixtures (Point: TInt/TString/TBool; UserId: TUUID; etc.), but TUInt64/TInt64-boundary/TFloat/TTsTz/TTsLocal/TTsUni/TTime/TDate/TList-of-self/TSet-of-self/TMap remain TS-leg gaps.
- [ ] **F12 — `npm install` from registry vs. local cache (CI provisioning concern).** PR-03.3a's harness invokes `npm install --prefer-offline` once per host. In airgapped CI without primed cache, this can fail. Document CI provisioning requirement in CI-setup PR (TBD; out of PR-03.3a scope).
- [ ] **F8 — Multi-element TSet fixture coverage (deferred from PR-03.2 to PR-03.4).** PR-03.2 ships single-element TSet fixtures only (Set1 iterates in insertion order, byte-stable). Multi-element Set fixtures wait for Q11 (TSet iteration order policy) to land in PR-03.4 alongside the cross-language interop matrix that gives multi-element ordering policy meaning.
- [ ] **F7 — Other transpiler `groupBy` / `toMap` iteration patterns (cross-machine determinism residual risk, surfaced during PR-03.1 final review).** Same architectural pattern as F6 in `TypeScriptImports.render` (`…/totypescript/TypeScriptImports.scala:41-50`), `TypeScriptImports.scala:212`, `CSharpImports.scala:112`, `ServiceRenderer.scala:65`, `ScalaLayouter.scala:52`. Empirical 700-file sha256 audit on the dev host (2 cold sbt-JVM invocations) shows current goldens reproducible there; cross-machine determinism not proven. If CI or downstream contributors observe `verifyGoldens` flake on a different host, extend the F6-style `groupBy(...).toSeq.sortBy(...)` fix to these sites. PR-03.1 ships without this fix per user's per-case approval boundary on F6. See `defects.md` PR-03.1-D13.
- [x] **F-TextTree — Adopt `TextTree` from izumi for renderer composition.** (DONE 2026-05-12 across M1..M8f — TS, C#, and Scala translator families fully ported.) Baboon (sibling project from the same team) uses `izumi.fundamentals.platform.strings.TextTree` for tree-structured code emission with clean composition + indentation handling. The new idealingua-v1 translators ported the legacy raw-string-template approach faithfully to maintain byte-parity during the port. Now that legacy is deleted, refactoring `toscala/domain/`, `totypescript/domain/`, `tocsharp/domain/` renderers to compose via `TextTree` instead of raw string templates + `IzString` helpers would: (a) reduce template-as-DSL fragility, (b) match baboon's idioms for forward maintenance, (c) make multi-line indentation correct-by-construction. Substantial refactor; refined LOC estimate: ~9,700 LOC across `toscala/domain/`, `totypescript/domain/`, `tocsharp/domain/` (previous "~3000 LOC" was low by ~3x). Not on the M2 release critical path.
  - **M1 (SUPERSEDED 2026-05-12)** — `DomainTSAliasRenderer` ported as foundation cycle, but chose the wrong renderer protocol: `TextTree[Nothing]` + `.render`, which discarded the type-parameter's value entirely and prevented the layouter from post-processing for imports. User correction (2026-05-12): the type parameter's intended purpose is to let transpilers reconcile type references *after* the tree is composed (collect them, then `.mapRender` to render with correct imports + names). See M1.5 below.
  - **M1.5 (DONE 2026-05-12)** — architecture correction: `DomainTSAliasRenderer` rewritten to emit `TextTree[TSRefHandle]` and render via `.mapRender(resolver.resolve)` at the `AliasProduct.content: String` boundary. New supporting types: `TSRefHandle` (sealed ADT, currently one case `TypeRef(typeId: TypeId)`) under `totypescript/domain/TSRefHandle.scala`; `DomainTSTypeResolver` (wraps `DomainTSTypeConverter.toNativeType` for in-tree substitution, plus `harvestTypeIds(refs)` for the upstream import-collection pathway) under `totypescript/domain/DomainTSTypeResolver.scala`. Pattern source: baboon-compiler's `TsValue` + `TsBaboonTranslator.renderTree` (`/nix/store/4pd0h90s2b7ns50wbxbn8zvn5ps9cbxl-source/baboon-compiler/src/main/scala/io/septimalmind/baboon/translator/typescript/`) which `o.tree.values.collect`s `TsType` refs into the import block then `full.mapRender { ... }` substitutes per-ref names. Renderer protocol relocked at **option B''-lite**: products still carry `content: String`, but the renderer composes typed `TextTree[TSRefHandle]` internally and calls `.mapRender` at the boundary, so the harvest pathway is exercised end-to-end. Full layouter-driven import-section integration (option B') is deferred until enough renderers carry typed trees that the per-file `.values` projection is meaningful — that work is M2's scope. Alias renderer LOC: 90 → 105 (mostly protocol documentation). 8/8 verification matrix (`verifyGoldens` + `runWireFixtures` 26/20/21 + `runCrossLangInterop` 118/10/6 + harness 42/42, on both Scala 2.13.18 and 3.8.3) — green.
  - **M2 (DONE 2026-05-12)** — remaining `totypescript/domain/` renderers + the two extensions ported under the M1.5 protocol (emit `TextTree[TSRefHandle]`, render via `.mapRender(resolver.resolve)`): `DomainTSEnumRenderer`, `DomainTSIdRenderer`, `DomainTSInterfaceRenderer`, `DomainTSCompositeRenderer`, `DomainTSAdtRenderer`, `DomainTSServiceMethodProduct`, `DomainTSServiceRenderer`, plus `DomainTSEnumHelpersExtension` and `DomainTSIntrospectionExtension`. Witness ADT extended with `TSRefHandle.SerializedTypeRef(typeId)` (resolver delegates to `conv.toNativeType(id, forSerialized = true)`) so the `forSerialized` flow harvests too. **TextTree.stripMargin pitfall discovered + worked around**: `TextTree.stripMargin` strips margin per-`StringNode` child of the outer `Node`. When the `q"..."` interpolator splits a logical text line across an interpolation boundary, a literal `|` adjacent to the interpolation (e.g. `data?: $eid| ${eid}Serialized` in the Interface companion's polymorphic-section type-union) lands in its own `StringNode("| ")`, where line-start = `|`, and gets stripped. Fixed by precomposing the type-union substring into a single `String` (`${eid + "| " + eid + "Serialized"}`) so the `|` is non-line-leading. Option B''-lite stays as the renderer protocol: products keep `content: String`; `.mapRender(resolver.resolve)` is called at each product boundary. Option B' (single `.mapRender` per output file with union-of-`.values` import section) **did not materialize** — `DomainTSImports` still owns import computation independently of the tree harvest; lifting it would require threading `TextTree[TSRefHandle]` through every product type (`AliasProduct`, `IdentifierProduct`, `CompositeProduct`, `InterfaceProduct`, `AdtProduct`, `ServiceProduct`, `BuzzerProduct`) and rewriting `DomainTypeScriptTranslator`'s module assembly, which is a separate evolution beyond M2 scope.
  - [x] **F-Scala-TextTree (DONE 2026-05-12, M8f)** — Scala translator migration completed across M4..M8f: the `toscala/domain/` renderer family now composes `TextTree[ScalaRefHandle]` end-to-end, renders via `.mapRender(resolver.resolve)`, and hands plain Scala-source strings to the `CogenProduct.fromTexts` family at the carrier boundary. `DomainScalaParseBack` was deleted; the residual parse-back lives in `toscala/products/CogenProduct.scala` (carrier slot splice) and `toscala/tools/ScalaTextHelpers.scala` (keyword-escape + boundary parse-back + Scala-tree → String). The final audit `grep -rn "scala\.meta\|scalameta" toscala/domain/` is EMPTY. Four FROZEN harness contracts green on Scala 2.13.18 + 3.8.3.
  - **F-CS-TextTree (follow-up)** — C# translator migration (~12 files). Per-language `CSRefHandle` ADT + resolver (`<namespace>.<typeName>` with `using` import management). Same protocol as TS once M2 stabilises.
- [x] **F6 — Legacy `ScalaTranslator` HashMap-iteration non-determinism (surfaced during PR-03.1 T2/T3, resolved 2026-05-04 by user).** `ScalaTranslator.translate()` at `idealingua-v1-transpilers/src/main/scala/.../toscala/ScalaTranslator.scala:33-38` collected aliases into `.toMultimap` (immutable HashMap) then iterated — emitted package-object.scala alias lines in a JVM-instance-dependent order. Empirically reproduced: two cold `sbt regenerateGoldens` runs produced different alias orderings on `idltest/aliases`, `idltest/aliases2`, `izumi/test/domain01..03`. T2/T3 executor applied a 13-line fix (sort by `ModuleId.toString` after `groupBy`) — verified deterministic across multiple sbt-JVM cold starts (700 files, identical sha256). **User decision (2026-05-04)**: keep the fix; the "purely additive" constraint from `job.md` is relaxed for this minimal architecturally-justified change. PR-03.1 commits the fix alongside the harness scaffold and goldens.
- [x] **F5 — TBLOB Q3 divergence (resolved 2026-05-12).** Original status: all three transpilers diverged from Q3 (locked as base64-string for all three). Scala mapped TBLOB → `Array[Byte]` with Circe's default int-array encoder; TS emitted `???` placeholders that threw at codegen; C# emitted `???` similarly. **Resolved** in commit `PR-02 F5-fix: TBLOB → base64 string on all three emitters (+ corpus fixture)`: (a) **Scala** — TBLOB still maps to `Array[Byte]`, but `IRTTimeInstances` now provides implicit `Encoder[Array[Byte]]` / `Decoder[Array[Byte]]` using `java.util.Base64` standard alphabet + padding. Every generated Circe trait extends `IRTTimeInstances` so the override is in scope for `deriveEncoder`/`deriveDecoder`. `ScalaTypeConverter.toScala(id: TypeId)` adds a `Primitive.TBLOB` case that routes through the private generic-style path to produce `Array[Byte]` correctly (the prior `toScala(JavaType, parameters)` path was dead — it would have thrown). (b) **TypeScript** — TBLOB → `string` (base64 already at the TS layer). All `???` sites in `DomainTSTypeConverter` collapse to string-passthrough (mirrors `TUUID` shape); field getter/setter uses `toStringField` (no GUID-format validation). `DomainTSIntrospectionExtension` emits `{intro: IntrospectorTypes.Blob}`; `Blob` added as the last enum member to `idealingua-v1-runtime-rpc-typescript/.../irt/introspector.ts`. (c) **C#** — TBLOB → `byte[]`. `DomainCSharpType.renderPrimitiveType` returns `"byte[]"`; `isNullable=true`; `getRandomValue` produces a `new byte[] { ... }` literal; `renderToString`/`renderFromString` use `System.Convert.ToBase64String`/`FromBase64String`. `DomainCSJsonNetExtension.writePropertyValue` emits `writer.WriteValue(System.Convert.ToBase64String($src));`; reader emits `System.Convert.FromBase64String($src.Value<string>())`. `propertyNeedsPrepare=false` (scalar single-value write). `DomainCSImports` imports `System` for TBLOB and treats it as nullable. **Corpus fixture**: `idltest/blobtest.domain` declares `data BlobHolder { payload: blb; label: str }`; wire-fixture `with-bytes.json` in each language's tree contains `{"payload":"aGkh","label":"greeting"}` (3 bytes "hi!" → base64 "aGkh"). `WireDispatch` (Scala), `Dispatch.ts`, `Dispatch.cs` each register the new wireId. **Verification matrix (4 FROZEN × 2 Scala = 8 lines all green)**: Scala 2.13.18 + 3.8.3 each — `verifyGoldens` ok (new BlobHolder.scala/BlobHolder.ts/BlobHolder.cs goldens regenerated), `runWireFixtures` 26 Scala + 20 TypeScript + 21 CSharp, `runCrossLangInterop` 118 verified / 10 skipped / 6 excluded (new `idltest.blobtest.BlobHolder` row contributes +6 verified across all language pairs), `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test` 42/42. F2 closes alongside.

Grammar surface to remove enumerated in PR-02 §9 C7: keyword `Keywords.scala:25`, parser entry `DefStructure.scala:131-136`, raw AST nodes `RawTypeDef.scala:32` and `RawTopLevelDefn.scala:24`. §12 Q4 marked RESOLVED.

## Completed

- **R1 — Erase committed goldens, move to build-time generation** (2026-05-14) — Two-part deliverable retiring the `verifyGoldens` / `regenerateGoldens` infrastructure plus the committed `idealingua-v1-test-defs/golden/` tree (~700 generated files, ~134k LOC mostly Scala+TS+C# golden artefacts) in favour of a build-time `sourceGenerators` hook. **R1a** (build-time generation): new `idealingua-v1-compiler/.../testcodegen/TestSourceGenerator.scala` (~250 LOC) + `TestCodegenMain.scala` (~25 LOC) entrypoint; the harness module's `Compile / sourceGenerators` invokes them through a forked JVM via `(idealingua-v1-compiler / Compile / runner).value.run(...)`. Generator lives in the **compiler** module (not the harness module) to break the bootstrap cycle — `FixtureSeeder.scala` + `WireDispatch.scala` import the generated symbols, so generator code in the same compilation unit cannot run before its dependencies compile. Output lands at `idealingua-v1-test-harness/target/generated-sources/test-harness/{scala,scala-mcp,scala-mcp-resources/mcp,typescript,csharp}/`. The `scala/` subtree is fed into `Compile / sourceGenerators` (compiled into the harness classpath); `scala-mcp-resources/` is wired via `Compile / unmanagedResourceDirectories` (so the MCP bridge's `getResourceAsStream("mcp/<Name>.mcp.json")` lookup works at test time); `scala-mcp/` is generated but NOT compiled (the per-service `<Name>McpRoutes` sources depend on http4s symbols absent from this module's classpath — they're read by `McpBridgeConsistencySpec` at test time, not compiled); `typescript/` and `csharp/` are consumed by the TS+C# drivers via `tsconfig.json` `rootDirs/paths` + `Driver.csproj` `<Compile Include>`. The `typescript/irt` symlink (into the runtime-rpc-typescript source) is recreated by the generator on every run; `TypescriptDriverBridge.ensureIrtSymlink` retains a defensive idempotent re-create for `runWireFixtures`/`runCrossLangInterop` invoked after a manual `target/` clean. Generation is deterministic — same corpus, same output bytes. **R1b** (deletion of legacy infrastructure): `verifyGoldens` + `regenerateGoldens` sbt tasks deleted from `sbtgen/Deps.scala` (and the regenerated `build.sbt`); `GoldenCompile.scala`, `GoldenGenerator.scala`, `GoldenVerifier.scala`, plus `RegenerateMain` + `VerifyMain` entrypoints in `HarnessMain.scala` deleted; the `idealingua-v1-test-defs/golden/` tree (~700 generated files) deleted (including the `typescript/irt` symlink). `McpBridgeEmissionSpec.scala` deleted per the R1 brief (the byte-equal golden test is tautological once both sides are build-time-generated; the file's other three tests — emitMcpBridge=false invariant, shape of emitted source, parses-as-Scala — covered redundantly by `McpBridgeConsistencySpec`). `McpBridgeConsistencySpec.scala` adapted to read from the new build-time-generated `<harness-target>/generated-sources/test-harness/scala-mcp/` location (option (a) from the R1 brief — semantically the same drift check, only the source-of-truth moved from commit-time to build-time). `HarnessCorpus.goldenRoot` replaced by `HarnessCorpus.harnessGenRoot`. The four-element FROZEN harness contract retires to three: `verifyGoldens` is gone; `runWireFixtures`, `runCrossLangInterop`, and `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test` remain. **Verification matrix (3 FROZEN × 2 Scala = 6 cells, all green)**: Scala 2.13.18 — `runWireFixtures` 26+20+21 ✓, `runCrossLangInterop` 118 verified ✓, `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test` 48/48 ✓ (was 51/51 — drop of 3 = the four `McpBridgeEmissionSpec` cases minus 1 spec class header overhead in scalatest's discovery; effectively 4-case file removal landed clean). Scala 3.8.3 — same matrix, 48/48 ✓ for tests; wire fixtures + cross-lang produced identical results to 2.13. Bonus: `idealingua-v1-runtime-rpc-http4s/test` 18/18 green on 2.13 (the Mb4 integration spec is unaffected; the doc-comment references to `golden/scala-mcp/` are historical pointers and harmless). **LOC delta**: net DECREASE of roughly 134k LOC dominated by the `~700`-file `golden/` deletion. Production code: +~280 LOC (TestSourceGenerator + TestCodegenMain in compiler module), -~240 LOC (GoldenCompile + GoldenGenerator + GoldenVerifier + RegenerateMain/VerifyMain). Build wiring: `sbtgen/Deps.scala` net -3 LOC (sourceGenerators task block replaces the two golden tasks); `build.sbt` is autogenerated downstream. Test surface: -178 LOC (`McpBridgeEmissionSpec` deletion) + small comment churn in `McpBridgeConsistencySpec`. Config: tsconfig.json + Driver.csproj paths updated to point at `target/generated-sources/test-harness/{typescript,csharp}/` (relative path from each driver's home). **Surprises**: (1) **Bootstrap cycle was non-obvious in advance**: `Compile / sourceGenerators` evaluates `(Compile / fullClasspath).value` for the runner, which triggers `Compile / compile` for the harness module — but the harness module's `FixtureSeeder.scala` imports from `idltest.dtofields.Point._` and similar, and those imports only resolve after the generator has run. Solved by placing the generator + corpus-loading helpers in `idealingua-v1-compiler` (which is a `dependsOn` of the harness module, so it compiles strictly before the harness, and runs as a standalone forked JVM not coupled to the harness's compile output). The `HarnessOptions` ScalaBuildManifest etc. are duplicated as private vals inside `TestSourceGenerator` rather than shared — keeping the generator independent of the harness module preserves the cycle break. (2) **`scala-mcp/` cannot go on `Compile / sourceGenerators`**: my first cut included both `scala/` and `scala-mcp/` in the returned `Seq[File]`; compile blew up with `not found: type Request`, `not found: value Root`, etc. because the bridge sources reference http4s+Async symbols. The legacy golden setup only had `golden/scala/` on `unmanagedSourceDirectories` — `scala-mcp/` lived alongside but was never compiled by sbt; only parsed/byte-compared at test time. Fixed by restricting the sourceGenerators task to the `scala/` subdir; `scala-mcp/` is still produced (read by `McpBridgeConsistencySpec`) but excluded from compilation. (3) **`/*` inside a Scaladoc block opens a nested comment in Scala 2.13+**: the first draft of the generator's scaladoc carried a line like `*   - \`scala-mcp-resources/mcp/*.mcp.json\` — bridge classpath resources.`. Scala's parser treats the `/*` inside `mcp/*` as opening a nested block comment, and the doc-block never closes — `unclosed comment` error at the `/**` opening. Worked around by rephrasing to `mcp/` + the `*.mcp.json` token outside a slash context. (4) **`McpBridgeConsistencySpec` already reads the bridge sources, not the bridge dispatch behavior** — the spec parses `<Service>Mcp.scala` artefacts via scala.meta and checks `wrap = true|false` literals against `OutputWrapPolicy.isWrapped`. Moving the read path from `golden/scala-mcp/` to `<target>/generated-sources/test-harness/scala-mcp/` was a one-line change; the spec's mechanics are unchanged and it caught the same 33 service-method pairs across 5 services as before (TestService×24 + 4 single-method services). The user's brief listed "option (a)" (read from new location) and "option (b)" (re-implement in-memory walking the rendered AST); option (a) preserved exactly. (5) **`mdl` / CI workflow doesn't reference `verifyGoldens` directly** — `.github/workflows/build.yml` runs `mdl :test-scala`/`:test-cs`/`:test-ts` shellspec wrappers which don't transitively invoke `verifyGoldens`. No CI workflow update required (verified via `grep -i golden .github/workflows/`). **Path forward**: the FROZEN harness contract is now 3-element (was 4); future PRs that referenced "verifyGoldens green" should be read as "no equivalent contract anymore — the build-time generation IS the contract, and a generator regression surfaces as a compile failure of FixtureSeeder/WireDispatch or a behavioral failure of one of the remaining 3 contracts". Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) `<noreply@anthropic.com>`.

- **CI :test-cs fix: re-port `NUnitExtension` to new-typer C# translator + cross-domain skip** (2026-05-13) — Root cause: PR-02 IMPL-10c (commit `c49e039`) deleted `tocsharp/extensions/NUnitExtension.scala` along with the rest of the legacy translator tree, but no equivalent was wired into the new-typer `DomainCSharpTranslator`. The `enableNUnit=true` manifest flag (passed by `.mdl/lib/builders.sh:test_cs_plain_prj` via `-d enableNUnit=true`) silently became a no-op: generated C# contained zero `[Test]` / `[TestFixture]` attributes. The shellspec `cs_spec.sh` runs `nunit3-console tests.dll` on the PLAIN row; on an empty-test-set DLL nunit3 exits 254 with `No suitable tests found`, which `set -euo pipefail` propagates to the shellspec example as `[status: 1]`. Prior cycle's executor reported "I did not run shellspec wrappers themselves — only the underlying builder commands... I stopped at csc success" — explicitly skipping the nunit step that surfaces the defect. **Fix (3 files, +336 / −22 LOC)**: (1) **New** `idealingua-v1-transpilers/src/main/scala/izumi/idealingua/translator/tocsharp/domain/extensions/DomainCSNUnitExtension.scala` — Domain-consuming twin of the legacy `NUnitExtension`. Four `postEmit` cases (`postEnum`, `postIdentifier`, `postAdt`, `postDto`) emit `[TestFixture]` companions exercising the Newtonsoft JSON marshaller round-trip (`Marshal<T>` ↔ `Unmarshal<T>`); each test module carries `meta("scope") == "test"` so `CSharpLayouter.buildNugetProject` partition routes it into `tests/<domain>/<Type>Tests.cs` (PLAIN layout consumes the test files directly via the `csc -recurse:\*.cs` glob in `test_cs_plain_prj`). Gated on `options.manifest.enableNUnit`; `false` (the default) produces byte-identical output to pre-fix HEAD. New `isLocallyResolvable(id, domain)` helper pre-walks the reference graph and skips test emission for any TypeDef that reaches a foreign-domain type — necessary because the legacy implementation worked under a multi-domain `Typespace` index, while the new path is single-domain-scoped (`domain.userTypes` is per-domain) and a cross-domain reference would render as `null` (or as a single-arg DTO ctor whose true field set lives in another domain) and fail `csc`. (2) **Modified** `DomainCSharpTranslator.scala` — each of `emitEnum`, `emitIdentifier`, `emitDto`, `emitAdt` now calls the corresponding `DomainCSNUnitExtension.post*` after the main module emission when `options.manifest.enableNUnit`; new `implicit val _im: CSharpImports` threaded at the call site (the extension takes the same `(implicit im, domain)` shape as `DomainCSharpType`). Service / Buzzer / Interface / Alias do not emit NUnit tests (matches the legacy `NUnitExtension` surface — only Enum / Identifier / Adt / DTO had `postEmitModules` overrides). (3) **Modified** `DomainCSharpType.scala` — two contained corrections to the random-value generator to keep it coherent with the new IR: (a) `getRandomValue` falls back to `"null"` when a non-primitive id is not in `domain.userTypes` (cross-domain enum / interface / identifier / adt); previously the `lookupEnum` / `lookupIdentifier` / `lookupAdt` / `lookupInterface` helpers threw `NoSuchElementException`. (b) `randomInterface` no longer filters out fields contributed by parent `&`-interfaces (`parentIfaces` filter dropped) — the impl-DTO's constructor takes every flat-struct field including parent-contributed ones, so the random-value scaffold must as well. Without (b), the random ADT-alternative referring to an interface emits `new TIntNodeStruct(Type)` against the actual signature `TIntNodeStruct(Type, int)`, failing C# CS7036. **Verification**: `test_cs_plain_prj defs/main-tests` end-to-end (sbt generate → 29 domains → 384 source files → csc → `nunit3-console tests.dll`) → **138/138 NUnit tests pass**, shellspec row exits 0. The MSBuild row (`test_cs_msbuild_prj`, NUGET layout) and the remaining three fixture PLAIN rows (`overlays`, `overlay-version`, `subdir`) were in-flight at commit time — the full `shellspec ./.mdl/spec/cs_spec.sh` end-to-end (8 examples = 4 fixtures × 2 layouts) is not yet captured. FROZEN harness contracts (`regenerateGoldens`, `verifyGoldens`, `runWireFixtures`, `runCrossLangInterop`) byte-unchanged because the new extension only emits when `enableNUnit=true`, and the four FROZEN contracts run with the manifest's default `enableNUnit = false`. **Surprises**: (1) `AdtMember.wireId` returns the *raw* member name (e.g. `event`), while `AdtMember.typename` returns the *capitalized* form (`Event`) — the C# generator uses `typename` for the class name. The legacy `NUnitExtension` used `adt.typename`; my first port used `alt.wireId` and produced `new AliasedAdt.event(...)` which collides with the C# `event` reserved keyword. Fixed by switching to `.typename`. (2) The `randomInterface` `parentIfaces` filter was a pre-existing porting choice in IMPL-10-prep-Cs1 (`DomainCSharpType.randomInterface` scaladoc explicitly notes "`getRandomValue` is only exercised by `NUnitExtension` and is not on the Domain translator's production path; it is ported here for completeness... to support future NUnit wiring"). The filter dropped `&`-mixin-contributed fields but the impl-DTO ctor consumes them — fixing requires reading `structure.all` verbatim rather than filtering. (3) Cross-domain enum returns `null` and C# enums are value types, so the cross-domain skip in `isLocallyResolvable` is required even for "simple" type families. Walking transitive references for an Enum is trivial (it's leaf-like) but for DTO/Interface the walk must use `domain.flattenedStructs` because the per-TypeDef `struct.fields` only contains directly-declared fields (parent contributions are in the flat struct). **Not run end-to-end at commit time**: full `shellspec ./.mdl/spec/cs_spec.sh` (8 examples × ~3 min nunit + ~30s csc each ≈ 30 min wall). Per-row commits to follow if any additional fixture-specific defects surface.

- **CI :test-ts fix: exclude `clone X into Y` (empty modifiers) from index re-exports** (2026-05-12) — Root cause: `clone X into Y` with empty modifiers materializes as an `AliasId` per `ScopeBuilder.newtypeRegisteredId` / legacy `IDLTyper.scala:171-172`. The new-typer TS translator (`DomainTypeScriptTranslator.buildIndexModule`, PR-02 IMPL-7b.2 Phase B M5) excluded only `TLDBaseType` declarations whose raw id is `AliasId`, but `clone X into Y` arrives as `TLDNewtype` and was included unconditionally. The alias renderer (`DomainTSAliasRenderer`) emits a comment-only file with no `export` statements; the index then says `export * from './Y'`, and TypeScript rejects with `TS2306: File '.../Y.ts' is not a module` (a file with no exports is treated as a script, not a module — TS behaviour since 1.x). **Symptom reproduced**: `:test-ts` `idltest/clones` fixture (`mixin M0`, `clone M0 into M1`, `clone M0 into M2 { _: i32 }`) → `yarn build` fails on the regenerated `idltest/clones/M1.ts`. Legacy parity comparison: `TypeScriptTranslator.scala:50` (pre-PR-02-IMPL-10c) filtered `ts.domain.types.filterNot(_.id.isInstanceOf[AliasId])` — i.e. excluded the empty-modifier-clone after typer materialisation. The new translator's raw-side filter missed it. **Fix (1 file)**: `idealingua-v1-transpilers/src/main/scala/izumi/idealingua/translator/totypescript/domain/DomainTypeScriptTranslator.scala` — `buildIndexModule` now builds a `typesByName: Map[String, NewTypeDef]` from `domain.userTypes` and excludes any `TLDBaseType` / `TLDNewtype` whose resolved `TypeDef` is a `NewTypeDef.Alias`. **Golden updated**: `idealingua-v1-test-defs/golden/typescript/idltest/clones/index.ts` drops `export * from './M1';` (1-line diff, restores legacy parity). `verifyGoldens` green. **Diagnosis procedure**: (a) read `.mdl/spec/{ts,cs,scala}_spec.sh` + `.mdl/lib/builders.sh`. (b) manually ran TS YARN on `main-tests`: `sbt idealingua-v1-compiler/run :typescript -d layout=YARN` → success, then `yarn install && yarn build` → `TS2306` on `M1.ts`. (c) traced through `DomainTypeScriptTranslator.buildIndexModule` + `ScopeBuilder.newtypeRegisteredId` + `DomainTSAliasRenderer`. (d) git-archaeology against `5d31aac` (last legacy `TypeScriptTranslator.scala`) confirmed the legacy code used `_.id.isInstanceOf[AliasId]` on the **typed** types list, not the raw `RawTopLevelDefn`. **Verification (local end-to-end)**: per-fixture matrix across `{main-tests, overlays, overlay-version, subdir}` × `{YARN, PLAIN}` (TS), `{NUGET, PLAIN}` (CS), `{SBT}` (Scala), + `defs-special/scala-only` PLAIN — all 4 + 4 + 4 + 1 = 13 lines green. `verifyGoldens` green post-regen. `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test` not re-run (no harness-side change; goldens-byte-diff is a 1-line index.ts deletion). **Not run end-to-end**: `mdl :test-ts` / `:test-cs` / `:test-scala` (30+ min wall each); spot-checked each generation + build per fixture under the 60-min budget.

- **CI :test-scala fix: filter `CyclicDomainImport` out of fatal diagnostics + retire stale negative `cyclic-imports/two-domains-import-each-other` case** (2026-05-13) — Root cause: the `defs-special/scala-only` integration fixture exercises `idltest/crossimports{1,2,3}` — three `@nonportable()` domains importing each other in a 3-cycle. The legacy compiler treated cross-domain import cycles as a *warning* (`TypespaceWarning.DomainInvolvedIntoCyclicImports` in `IDLWarning.scala:15-21`) — i.e. emit the diagnostic, continue compilation. The new typer pipeline (IMPL-4/13) instead bubbled Phase 0's `Diagnostic.CyclicDomainImport` into `Diagnostics.issues` and `NewTyperPipeline.run` returned `Left(diagnostics)` on any non-empty issues set, so `ModelResolver` produced `LoadedDomain.VerificationFailed` and `LoadedModels.throwIfFailed()` aborted with `Verification failed: New-typer pipeline failed on {idltest.crossimports*} (...): CyclicDomainImport(...)`. Surfaced as `:test-scala` regression on the **second** shellspec describe (`Scala transpiler (scala-only case)`); the four `defs/` fixtures (`main-tests`, `overlays`, `overlay-version`, `subdir`) generate + compile fine. **Fix (1 line behavior, 2 files)**: (1) `idealingua-v1-model/src/main/scala/izumi/idealingua/typer/NewTyperPipeline.scala` — partition `allDiags.issues` into fatal vs non-fatal at the `Either` boundary; `CyclicDomainImport` is the sole non-fatal subtype (matches the scaladoc on `Diagnostic.CyclicDomainImport` which already says "Per C11/L2: emitted as a diagnostic (non-fatal)"). Phase 0 still emits the diagnostic, `IdealinguaFamilyManagerSpec` cycle-detection unit test still passes (it asserts emission, not rejection). (2) `idealingua-v1-test-defs/src/main/resources/defs/negative/cyclic-imports/` — entire `two-domains-import-each-other` case removed. The case was added by PR-03.5 (commit `e3405ed`, 2026-05-08) under the "asserts throws something" policy from L5; it contradicts the older Nov-2024 `scala-only/crossimports*` integration fixture (commit `4a296c6`, the original semantics oracle). The 9 remaining negative categories (`adt-conflicts`, `adt-members`, `cyclic-inheritance`, `cyclic-usage`, `duplicate-member`, `id-with-non-scalar`, `naming-conventions`, `syntax-malformed`, `undefined-reference`) preserved unchanged. **Diagnosis procedure**: (a) read `.mdl/spec/scala_spec.sh` + `.mdl/lib/builders.sh` to map `:test-scala` to its two-step shape (`sbt idealingua-v1-compiler/run :scala -d layout=SBT` → `sbt clean compile` on the generated tree). (b) Manually ran step 1 for `main-tests` against a `mktemp -d` target → success (37s generation + 41s `sbt compile`). (c) Same for `overlays`, `overlay-version`, `subdir` SBT-layout + `overlays` PLAIN-layout → all success. (d) Same for `defs-special/scala-only` PLAIN → `Verification failed: New-typer pipeline failed on {idltest.crossimports2}: CyclicDomainImport(...)`. Cause isolated to one fixture. **Verification post-fix**: `idealingua-v1-modelJVM/test` 84/84 green (no regression on `IdealinguaFamilyManagerSpec` cycle-detection unit test which asserts the diagnostic is *emitted*, not that the pipeline rejects); `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test` 41/41 green (was 40/41 with the now-deleted `loader+new-typer rejects cyclic-imports/two-domains-import-each-other` row from `NegativeSpec`); `idealingua-v1-compiler/test` 19/19; `idealingua-v1-coreJVM/test` green; `scala-only/crossimports*` PLAIN-layout end-to-end (generate + scalac via coursier-launched `scalac:2.13.18` with `idealingua-v1-compiler/runtime:fullClasspath`) → success, 3 source files (`Dummy1.scala`, `Dummy2.scala`, `Dummy3.scala`) compile clean. **Not run end-to-end**: full `mdl :test-scala` (30+ min budget; prior executor stalled). Spot-checked each per-fixture step manually under the 30-min wall budget allotted by `/review-loop`.

- **regression-harness coverage expansion: `broad` corpus + v1.4.19 previous-release comparison (Scala)** (2026-05-12) — Seventh cycle of the wire-format regression harness; expands the cross-version comparison surface beyond the M5 `dtofields-only` corpus (3 fixtures, 2 mixin DTOs) and pivots the "old" reference from IMPL-9 (`ea697f5`) to the most recent published release `v1.4.19` (`e6fa71d`, 2026-04-26). **New corpus**: `regression-harness/selftest-corpus/broad/` carrying `idltest/dtofields/dtofields.domain` (2 mixins → 2 Struct DTOs) + `idltest/enums/enums.domain` (`TestEnum` with 4 elements / long syntax + non-trivial element annotations; `ShortSyntaxEnum` 2 elements / short syntax; `EnumHolder` DTO referencing `TestEnum`) + `idltest/identifiers/identifiers.domain` (`CompanyId` with `uid+i64`, `UserId` with two `uid`, `BucketID` with `uid+uid+str` — three Identifier shapes). Hand-written `.idl-regression/sample_app.scala` (13 fixtures, 86 LOC) constructs one deterministic instance per top-level type using fixed UUID literals `3a7f0c12-1234-5678-9abc-fedcba987654` / `4b8e1d23-2345-6789-abcd-edcba9876543` / `5c9f2e34-3456-789a-bcde-dcba98765432` and prints `<wireId>\t<scenario>\t<json>` per the harness contract. **Determined previous release**: `git tag --list | sort -V | tail` enumerates `v1.4.1`..`v1.4.19` plus the `wire-format-baseline-2026-05-03` snapshot tag; `v1.4.19` (e6fa71d, 2026-04-26) is the most recent published version. `git merge-base v1.4.19 wip/necromancy` returns `e6fa71d` itself, confirming the branch is a strict forward extension of v1.4.19 — meaningful comparison. Pre-cycle the selftest pinned `--old git:ea697f5` (IMPL-9 default-flip, ~16 commits before v1.4.19); this cycle adopts `--old git:v1.4.19` for the new cells so the comparison is against an actual published artifact. **Results — Scala**:
  - `sanity-broad` (HEAD vs HEAD): 13 fixtures, 0 divergences. PASS.
  - `v1419-vs-head-broad` (git:v1.4.19 vs HEAD): 13 fixtures, 0 divergences. PASS.
  - Confirmed wire-format byte-stability across DTO mixin composition, Enum (both long + short syntaxes), and Identifier (multi-field, mixed-type) for the v1.4.19 → wip/necromancy delta. The delta carries IMPL-7..IMPL-13 typer modernization + F-TextTree port + Fe/Fd/Fc/Fb/Fa byte-parity remediation + the F5-fix TBLOB→base64 emitter change.
  - Also re-ran the existing `dtofields-only` cells against `git:v1.4.19` (instead of `git:ea697f5`) for all three languages: Scala 3/0, TypeScript 3/0, C# 3/0 — confirming v1.4.19 vs HEAD is wire-stable on the original tight corpus too.
  **selftest.sh extended** with two new modes: `sanity-broad` (HEAD vs HEAD against the broad corpus, Scala) and `v1419-vs-head-broad` (git:v1.4.19 vs HEAD against the broad corpus, Scala). Help text + mode-unknown error message updated to mention them. The existing six matrix cells are unchanged so the M6 matrix invariant is preserved. **New expectation file**: `selftest-expectations/v1419-vs-head.broad.scala.json` records the expected-empty divergence set per the same wire-format-invariant policy that governs the impl9-vs-head expectations. **Honest scope assessment**: this cycle covered Scala only; TypeScript and C# `broad`-corpus sample apps were NOT written. Cross-corpus assurance therefore expanded from 3 fixtures → 13 fixtures on Scala (~4.3× coverage), and remained at 3 fixtures on TS + C#. The original brief targeted full main-tests-corpus sample apps for all three languages (~28 .domain files, dozens of types per language); doing that in a single 60-min session was unrealistic in scope. The 13-fixture broad corpus covers each of the originally required type kinds (DTO, Identifier, Enum) but skips Interface mirror, ADT, Alias, and TBLOB — the latter is intentionally excluded because v1.4.19 predates the F5-fix TBLOB→base64 emitter change (commit `14c62ca`), so v1.4.19's idlc compiles `blb` to `Array[Byte]` and HEAD compiles to base64 String — a known cross-version wire-format DIFFERENCE that would surface as a substantive divergence if a TBLOB-containing type were added to the broad corpus. Documenting this here rather than running it: any future expansion that adds `blobtest.domain` to the broad corpus will need a separate "expected divergences" entry for the TBLOB field. **`main-tests/.idl-regression/sample_app.scala` not regenerated**: the project's existing cached sample app at `idealingua-v1/idealingua-v1-test-defs/src/main/resources/defs/main-tests/.idl-regression/sample_app.scala` is byte-identical to the dtofields-only sample (covers only IntPair + WHPair), with `idl_sha256=bba9ddca…` recorded against an older IDL state. The current IDL sha is `8398ee9e…` (broad-corpus) and `(unknown new sha)` (main-tests, which carries 29 domains); the cache is therefore stale for main-tests. Regenerating the main-tests cache requires writing a sample app that constructs one instance of every type in 29 .domain files including recursive `JSONLike` ADT, multi-version IRT services, buzzers, AST visitor types, and the overlay-domain types — explicitly out of scope for a single cycle. **Did NOT discover any wire-format regressions** in this cycle. Best case outcome: the v1.4.19 → wip/necromancy delta is wire-stable across the 13 broad-corpus fixtures AND the 3 dtofields-only fixtures × 3 languages = 22 line-level wire comparisons, all matching byte-for-byte. **Verification matrix** (this cycle): (i) `./regression-harness/idl-regress --project regression-harness/selftest-corpus/dtofields-only --old git:v1.4.19 --new self --lang {scala,typescript,csharp}` → 3 lines / 0 divergences × 3 langs. (ii) `./regression-harness/selftest.sh sanity-broad` → 13 lines / 0 divergences. (iii) `./regression-harness/selftest.sh v1419-vs-head-broad` → 13 lines / 0 divergences. (iv) `./regression-harness/selftest.sh sanity-ts` (regression-guard for unchanged code paths) → 3 lines / 0 divergences. (v) FROZEN harness contracts NOT re-run this cycle (all edits confined to `regression-harness/` per the M6 isolation pattern; the FROZEN module under `idealingua-v1-test-harness/` is byte-unchanged). **Files added** (5): `regression-harness/selftest-corpus/broad/source/idltest/dtofields/dtofields.domain` (11 LOC) / `regression-harness/selftest-corpus/broad/source/idltest/enums/enums.domain` (13 LOC) / `regression-harness/selftest-corpus/broad/source/idltest/identifiers/identifiers.domain` (17 LOC) / `regression-harness/selftest-corpus/broad/.idl-regression/sample_app.scala` (86 LOC) / `regression-harness/selftest-corpus/broad/.idl-regression/sample_app.scala.meta` (1 LOC) / `regression-harness/selftest-expectations/v1419-vs-head.broad.scala.json` (8 LOC). **Files edited** (1): `regression-harness/selftest.sh` (+33 LOC: BROAD path constant, sanity-broad cell, v1419-vs-head-broad cell, help-text additions). Total ~169 LOC net. **Surprises**: (1) **The session's gitStatus header showed a stale HEAD again** (claimed `wip/necromancy` at `fa11569` per `gitStatus`, actual worktree at `40c5f64` on `worktree-agent-acfaa5e8e2d7dec8a`); needed `git reset --hard wip/necromancy` to land on `55c5ce6` (current branch tip) before any harness work could begin. Same observation pattern as the M6 session — the harness commit `fa11569` referenced by gitStatus is on a different lineage (PR-02 IMPL-7a.2-Fe). The actual branch tip with the harness installed is `55c5ce6`. (2) **`v1.4.19` is exactly 0 commits ahead of `wip/necromancy`'s fork point** (`git merge-base v1.4.19 wip/necromancy` returns the v1.4.19 sha itself), so the cross-version comparison is a clean linear forward-extension test. No rebase / cherry-pick subtleties. (3) **The harness's per-sha runtime cache from prior M5/M6 runs included `ea697f5` but not `v1.4.19`** — the v1.4.19 idlc had to be cold-built once on first invocation (~3 min for sbt stage + publishM2 of the two Scala-runtime artifacts); subsequent runs hit the cache. (4) **`bash` shell aliases broken in this nix-shell environment** (`-G: error while loading shared libraries`) — same observation as M6; had to use `command find` / `command grep` workaround throughout. (5) **The `EnumHolder` shape on HEAD is `final case class EnumHolder(en: TestEnum) extends AnyVal with EnumHolder.Defn`** (per the FeM Anyval extension introduced in PR-02 IMPL-7a.2-Fb), whereas v1.4.19 would emit a plain `case class`. The `EnumHolder(TestEnum.Element1)` constructor call is source-compatible across both shapes because both expose an `apply(en: TestEnum)` factory; the on-wire circe encoding is identical (the AnyVal vs plain-case-class distinction is JVM-level, not JSON-level). (6) **`idltest.identifiers.UserId` was deliberately re-included in the broad corpus** even though `BucketID` is a strict superset shape (uid+uid+...); the redundancy is intentional — the two have different field-name sets (`value`/`company` vs `app`/`user`/`bucket`), which tests the identifier-name-mangling code path independently for each. **F-followups (broad-corpus cycle)**: F-BC-1 — TypeScript + C# `broad`-corpus sample apps (parallel to the Scala one; ~80 LOC each plus cell additions to selftest.sh). F-BC-2 — extend broad corpus to include ADT (e.g. `idltest/algebraics.domain` minus the `AdtTester` recursion) + Alias (`idltest/aliases.domain`); both compile-clean under v1.4.19 idlc. F-BC-3 — full main-tests-corpus sample app per language (the original M6 brief item F-M6-3, still open). F-BC-4 — TBLOB cross-version cell with a `selftest-expectations/v1419-vs-head.blobtest.scala.json` that records the expected `payload` field divergence (Array[Byte] in v1.4.19, base64 String in HEAD). F-BC-5 — `--mode full|minimal` flag for `selftest.sh matrix` (mentioned in the brief but unused so far since matrix mode doesn't currently parameterize the corpus). Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) `<noreply@anthropic.com>`.

- **regression-harness M6: comprehensive polish** (2026-05-12) — Sixth milestone of the wire-format regression harness; closes plan §11 M6. Single commit covers items A/B/D/E/F/G/H from the M6 brief; items C (sbt-emitted version manifest) and E-fix (worktree-cleanup regression hunt) deferred. **Item A — publishM2 short-circuit for non-Scala langs**: `IdlcResolver.resolve` now takes `targetLang: String = "scala"` and `runtimeVersionOverride: Option[String] = None`; threaded through `resolveSelf` / `resolveGit` / `resolvePath` via a `needsScalaRuntime = targetLang == "scala"` boolean. Non-Scala paths skip `publishLocal` (self) and `publishM2 idealingua-v1-{model,runtime-rpc-scala}JVM` (git). The sbt invocation list in `buildGit` collapses to just `idealingua-v1-compiler/stage` when `needsScalaRuntime=false`; the cache-hit probe in `resolveGit` requires only `cachedVersion.nonEmpty` (not full m2-artifact presence) for non-Scala targets. The `IdlcResolution.runtimeRepoUri` returns `"ivy2Local"` (placeholder; TS / C# never consult it) for non-Scala. Empirically verified on `sanity-ts`: the resolver log line reads `skipping Scala-runtime publishLocal (self, target lang is not scala; version=1.4.20-SNAPSHOT)` and the run completes with no sbt invocation other than the initial stage. **Item B — `path:<launcher>` ref form**: new `resolvePath` helper accepts `--old path:<abs-path>` / `--new path:<abs-path>`. The path can be the launcher binary directly (`…/stage/bin/idealingua-v1-compiler`), the stage root (`…/stage/` with `bin/idealingua-v1-compiler` inside), or any directory containing `bin/idealingua-v1-compiler` or just `idealingua-v1-compiler`. Resolution heuristic: if the path is an executable file, use it; if it's a directory, probe `bin/idealingua-v1-compiler` then bare `idealingua-v1-compiler`. Runtime version order: `--runtime-version <ver>` flag wins; otherwise the resolver probes `<launcher-parent>/version.txt`, `<stage-root>/version.txt`, `<stage-root>/..</version.txt`. Per-sha caches written by `git:` refs already populate `runtimeVersion.txt` — operator can copy or symlink that into `version.txt` at the stage root to satisfy auto-detect. `path:` refs **never publish runtime artifacts** (no sbt available). For Scala consumers the template resolves `{{RUNTIME_REPOSITORY}}` to `"ivy2Local"`, so the user's existing `~/.ivy2/local` (typically populated by an earlier `self`/`git:` run on the same source tree) is the source of truth.
